## 2026-07-24 — Wrap the app in Capacitor for Android (configuration and build only)

**Context:** PHASE0.md's printer section already anticipated this ("wrap the PWA with Capacitor" if the P1's Bluetooth share-sheet flow proves painful). This task is the ground floor only — install, configure, and prove a debug APK builds — with the printer SDK, Capgo live updates, and any UI/business-logic change explicitly deferred.

**Inspected and reported before coding, per the task's gate:**
- No existing bundle id, reverse-DNS domain, or native project anywhere in the repo — `appId`/`appName` were an open choice. Picked `com.batuexport.app` / "BATU EXPORT", the latter matching the existing PWA manifest's `name`/`short_name` exactly (`vite.config.ts`) so the native app's label is identical to the installed-PWA label already in use.
- Environment: Android Studio and the SDK are installed (`android-36.1` platform, `build-tools 36.0.0`, platform-tools/`adb`), with a bundled JDK 21 at `Android Studio\jbr`. Neither `java` nor `adb` are on `PATH`, and no emulator AVD or physical device was connected — so a Gradle build was attemptable here, but launching/running the app was not. Reported this plainly before proceeding rather than guessing.
- `App.tsx` uses `BrowserRouter` (client-side history routing); `supabase.ts` reads `VITE_SUPABASE_URL`/`VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` from `import.meta.env`, currently populated by Netlify's build-time environment and, locally, by the gitignored `.env` every developer already has for `npm run dev`.

**App id/name (user's call, reported):** `com.batuexport.app`, "BATU EXPORT" — set in `capacitor.config.ts` and matching `MainActivity`'s package.

**Bundled locally, not pointed at Netlify.** `capacitor.config.ts` has no `server` block at all — Capacitor's default behavior with `server` unset is to serve `webDir` from the bundled local assets via its own local server (`https://localhost`, not a real network request), which is what makes the shell load with zero signal. `webDir` points at `dist-native/` (see below), and Capacitor's default `https` scheme is what allows `getUserMedia` (a secure-context API) to work fully offline in the WebView — nothing extra needed for that.

**PWA service worker — separated by build mode, not by touching the plugin's own config.** `vite.config.ts` now takes the `mode` Vite already passes to `defineConfig`'s function form: when `mode === 'capacitor'`, the `VitePWA` plugin is skipped entirely (`.filter(Boolean)` over the plugins array) and `build.outDir` becomes `dist-native` instead of `dist`. Every other mode (`npm run dev`, plain `npm run build`) is byte-for-byte unchanged — same plugin, same Workbox config, same `dist/`. A new `build:native` script (`tsc -b && vite build --mode capacitor`) is the only thing that ever passes that mode. Verified, not assumed: built both outputs back to back — `dist/` still contains `sw.js`, `workbox-*.js`, `manifest.webmanifest`, `registerSW.js`; `dist-native/` contains none of them, just `index.html` + hashed JS/CSS/wasm. `npm run cap:sync` (`build:native` then `npx cap sync android`) is the one command that gets a fresh build into the native shell.

**Android permissions.** Capacitor's default template already declares `INTERNET`; added `CAMERA` (`AndroidManifest.xml`) since barcode scanning feeds `getUserMedia` frames to `BarcodeDetector` in-page, no native camera plugin involved — Capacitor's stock `BridgeActivity`/`WebChromeClient` forwards the WebView's `getUserMedia` permission prompt to Android's runtime permission system automatically once the manifest declares it, so no custom native code was needed. Also added `<uses-feature android:name="android.hardware.camera" android:required="false">` so a camera-less device isn't filtered out of installability — manual barcode entry is a fully supported fallback already. Checked the final **merged** manifest from the Gradle build (not just the source one) to confirm nothing extra got pulled in by a dependency: `INTERNET`, `CAMERA`, the `uses-feature`, and one auto-injected `…DYNAMIC_RECEIVER_NOT_EXPORTED_PERMISSION` signature permission that AndroidX Core adds unconditionally on target SDK 33+ (Android 13's dynamic-broadcast-receiver export rule) — boilerplate every modern Capacitor app carries, not a real user-facing permission and not something this task added. Nothing Bluetooth-related, as instructed.

**Env vars — no new mechanism, reused what already exists.** Vite loads plain `.env` for *every* mode, including a custom one (`.env.[mode]` files are additive on top, not a replacement) — so the same gitignored `.env` a developer already has for `npm run dev` also feeds `build:native` with zero extra configuration. Verified, not assumed: after `build:native`, grepped the real `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` value from `.env` against the built `dist-native/assets/*.js` bundle and confirmed it's inlined (Vite bakes `import.meta.env.VITE_*` in at build time). No secret is committed anywhere — `.env` was already gitignored, and the new `android/local.properties` (machine-local `sdk.dir` path, not a secret but still machine-specific) is excluded by Capacitor's own generated `android/.gitignore`, which was inspected and left as-is: it already correctly excludes `local.properties`, `build/`, `.gradle/`, the copied `app/src/main/assets/public` web bundle, and the generated `capacitor.config.json`/`capacitor.plugins.json` — confirmed with a dry-run `git add -n android/` (53 files staged, all genuine source: manifest, Gradle files, wrapper, `MainActivity.java`, default template resources — no build output, no `local.properties`).

**Build attempted here, and it succeeded.** Pointed Gradle at the installed toolchain without touching any system-wide setting: `JAVA_HOME` set process-scoped for the one build invocation (`Android Studio\jbr`, JDK 21), `android/local.properties` written with `sdk.dir` pointing at the local SDK (a project-local, gitignored file — the standard place for this, not a system change). `./gradlew.bat assembleDebug` from `android/`: **BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 11m 31s**, `android/app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk` (5.2 MB) confirmed on disk and correctly gitignored. `npm install` added Capacitor with **zero new `npm audit` findings** — the 3 pre-existing vulnerabilities (`exceljs`/`uuid`/`fast-uri`) are unchanged, unrelated to this task.

**What I could not verify here, and why:** no `adb devices` and no emulator AVD were available (confirmed, not assumed), so I could not install or launch the APK myself — login, role screen, and camera scanning all need a real device. Exact steps are in the report below, not guessed.

**Flagged, not fixed (out of scope — no UI/routing change made):** `BrowserRouter` uses the History API; Capacitor's local WebView server does not automatically fall back to `index.html` for an arbitrary sub-path the way Netlify's SPA redirect does. In the ordinary case this is a non-issue — the app always cold-starts at `/` and every in-app navigation is client-side `pushState`, exactly like a fresh browser tab hitting the Netlify root today. The one scenario worth testing on-device: if Android reclaims the app's process while it's backgrounded on a sub-route (e.g. `/menejer/chiqim`) and the WebView's navigation state is restored to that URL on relaunch rather than the app cold-starting at `/`, the local server has no file at that path. Not reproduced or fixed here — App.tsx routing was explicitly read-only per the task's scope. If it does turn up on-device, the fix is small (either a WebView-state-restore opt-out so the app always cold-starts at `/`, or a local server fallback rule) and shouldn't need a router change.

**Also flagged, cosmetic, not fixed:** `npx cap add android` scaffolds Capacitor's generic default launcher icon and splash screen (blue/white placeholder), not BATU EXPORT branding. Out of scope for a "configuration and build only" task — worth a follow-up once real launcher/splash assets are wanted.

**Verification:** `npx tsc -b --noEmit` clean (covers the new `capacitor.config.ts`, added to `tsconfig.node.json`'s `include`). `npx oxlint` clean — the 3 warnings present are pre-existing (`AuthProvider.tsx`'s known dual-export warning, two `usePendingRewash.ts` exhaustive-deps warnings), none introduced by this task. Unit suite: 79/79 passed. Full Playwright suite run once, dev server left running throughout (not stopped mid-run): **4/4 passed, including `report-effective-qty-parity.spec.ts`.** Live-browser sanity check of the untouched web/dev path: zero console errors, login screen rendered correctly.

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** the Detonger P1 printer plugin, Capgo live updates, any UI or business-logic change, iOS.

## 2026-07-24 — P1 printer integration: native Capacitor plugin, pairing, and printing from both barcode screens

**Context:** the Capacitor Android wrap (previous entry) deliberately left the P1 printer as a future step. This is that step — a native plugin wrapping DothanTech's LPAPI SDK (`vendor/printer-sdk/`), printer pairing persisted across sessions, and label printing wired into both `Barcode1Display.tsx` and `Barcode2Display.tsx`.

**Inspected and reported before coding, per the task's gate.** The PDF documentation and the demo disagreed in one place — the callback method is `onStateChange`, not `onStateChanged` as the PDF prose has it — resolved by decompiling the real jar (`javap` against `android/app/libs/LPAPI-2026-01-08-R.jar`) rather than trusting either source blindly. The same jar-level check also surfaced that `discovery()`/`stopDiscovery()` are real, callable methods entirely absent from the PDF's own method chapters (only the demo uses them), and gave the exact `PrintFailReason` enum names (`No_Paper`, `CoverOpened`, `TphOpened`, `VolTooLow`, `Disconnected`, …) needed for requirement E's specific-message mapping. No other divergence from the reported plan — proceeded as approved.

**A — the plugin interface, as built** (`android/app/src/main/java/com/batuexport/app/P1PrinterPlugin.java`, `src/lib/p1Printer.ts`):
```ts
interface PrinterInfo { address: string; name: string }
interface PrintLabelOptions { barcode: string; serial: string; typeName: string; calibreLabel?: string; weightKg: number; clientName: string }
interface P1PrinterPlugin {
  listPrinters(): Promise<{ printers: PrinterInfo[] }>
  selectPrinter(options: { address: string }): Promise<{ connected: boolean }>
  printLabel(options: PrintLabelOptions): Promise<{ success: boolean; reason?: PrintFailureReason }>
}
```
`calibreLabel` is optional — Barcode #1 (raw material) has no calibre yet, and the native layout just omits that line rather than leaving a dangling separator. Everything LPAPI reports asynchronously via `Callback`; every method holds its `PluginCall` open until the matching callback fires (`onStateChange` for connect, `onPrintProgress` for print), guarded by a timeout (10s connect, 18s print) that always resolves or rejects the JS side rather than leaving it hanging. A `connectionChange` event (`notifyListeners`) fires on every `onStateChange`, independent of whether JS is waiting on a call, so the UI's connection dot updates even when nothing triggered it directly (e.g. the printer going idle on its own). Persistence is entirely JS-side via `@capacitor/preferences` (`src/lib/usePrinter.ts`) — the plugin itself never touches storage, keeping the native surface to exactly three methods.

**B — 40×30mm layout, as built.** No rotation (`startJob(40, 30, 0)`) — the stock is already landscape at those dimensions. Left-aligned, top to bottom: barcode bars with LPAPI's own built-in human-readable text (~10mm) · serial (~3.5mm, medium prominence) · `typeName · calibreLabel` or just `typeName` when no calibre (~3mm) · `weightKg kg` (~4mm, prominent) · `clientName`, truncated with an ellipsis past 22 characters, no shrink-to-fit (~3mm). **The layout now genuinely exists in two places, as expected:** `P1PrinterPlugin.java`'s native drawing is authoritative for what actually prints; `barcodeLabel.ts`'s canvas/JsBarcode renderer is an explicit preview, used for the on-screen SVG, the web share/download fallback, and nothing else. They were already going to diverge (native draws its own barcode value as text under the bars; the web canvas draws a separate, larger code-value line since JsBarcode has no built-in human-readable text) — resized `barcodeLabel.ts`'s `LABEL_MM` from 50×30 to 40×30 to at least match stock dimensions, since printing the old 50×30 PNG via the web share sheet onto real 40×30 stock would have laid out wrong. Barcode #1's native label also drops the `date` field entirely (present on the web preview, not part of the raw-material fields the task specified) — another deliberate, documented difference between the two, not a bug.

**C — Bluetooth permissions**, exactly as specified: `BLUETOOTH_SCAN`+`BLUETOOTH_CONNECT` (with `neverForLocation`, since the printer is classic Bluetooth SPP and its scan results are never used for location — this avoids ALSO needing location permission on 12+) on Android 12+; `BLUETOOTH`+`BLUETOOTH_ADMIN`+`ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION`+`ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION` (via `maxSdkVersion="30"`, a manifest-level no-op above that rather than branching the manifest itself) below. Requested through Capacitor's own `@Permission`/`@PermissionCallback` framework (two aliases, `bluetoothModern`/`bluetoothLegacy`, picked by `Build.VERSION.SDK_INT`), not hand-rolled — denial rejects with a stable `PERMISSION_DENIED` code that `src/lib/p1Printer.ts` maps to a fixed Uzbek message, never a silent no-op.

**D — printer selection and reconnection.** `PrinterStatus.tsx` (a small connection dot + tap-to-scan-and-select panel, rendered directly next to the print button in both display components, not a separate settings screen) is the "pick once" UI; `usePrinter.ts` persists the choice via `@capacitor/preferences` and re-issues one `selectPrinter` call per app session on mount so a saved printer reconnects transparently before the first print is ever attempted. Auto-reconnect-on-print lives natively, not in JS: `P1PrinterPlugin.doPrintLabel` checks `getPrinterState()` first, and if the printer's gone idle/disconnected but a printer was selected earlier this session (`currentAddress` still set), silently reconnects to that same address and only then draws — surfacing an error only if the reconnect itself fails. Ombor never sees a reconnect screen; they just tap print. The connection dot is a live `useSyncExternalStore`-backed store (not per-component state) specifically because `Barcode2Display` renders once per pallet row — many simultaneous instances all need to reflect the same connection status, not independent copies.

**E — failure reasons.** `mapFailReason` in the plugin collapses LPAPI's 25 `PrintFailReason` values to 5: `NO_PAPER` (`No_Paper`/`No_Label`/`Usedup_Label`), `COVER_OPEN` (`CoverOpened`/`TphOpened`/`LabelCanOpend`), `LOW_BATTERY` (`VolTooLow`), `NOT_CONNECTED` (`Disconnected`), `OTHER` (everything else — ribbon/temperature/rotation/timeout-adjacent states a warehouse worker can't act on any more specifically than "try again"). `src/lib/p1Printer.ts` maps each to a fixed Uzbek message shown inline under the print button.

**F — both label types, auto-print, and reprint.** Barcode #2 already had the exact shape this needed: `defaultOpen` on first appearance after a save (`OmborTayyorTab.tsx`'s `lastBarcode`), collapsed-by-default in every historical pallet row. Added the identical moment to Barcode #1, which had no `defaultOpen` concept at all before this task — `OmborIntakeTab.tsx` gained a `justAccepted` state (mirrors `lastBarcode` exactly) set right after `handleAccept` mints `barcode1`. Both components: native available → auto-print fires once per distinct barcode value (not once per component instance — both `OmborTayyorTab`/`OmborIntakeTab` reuse the same mounted instance across multiple saves in one session, so a mount-only guard would have silently stopped auto-printing after the first) via an effect keyed on the barcode string; the same "Chop etish" button is the reprint action everywhere, including older pallets/serials from prior sessions — reprint never depended on when the row was created, only on the barcode value still being on screen.

**Real regression found and fixed via the e2e suite, not assumed safe from inspection:** the first full-suite run (after an unrelated dev-server disruption — see below — was corrected) still failed `rewash-hard-gate.spec.ts` on a genuine `strict mode violation`, not a flake: unconditionally honoring `defaultOpen` in `Barcode1Display` meant its label-preview `div` (class `rounded-md border-2 …`) now rendered immediately after every Ombor accept, even on the web build with nothing to auto-print — and that div's `rounded-md` class collided with the spec's own `div.rounded-md` locator for the accepted-serial row, resolving to 2 elements instead of 1. Fixed by only honoring `defaultOpen` when `isP1PrinterAvailable()` — on web, Barcode #1's panel stays collapsed on accept exactly as it did before this task, since there's no auto-print to justify opening it. `Barcode2Display`'s equivalent pre-existing web auto-open (already shipped before this task, `OmborTayyorTab.tsx`'s own `lastBarcode`) was left untouched — it isn't causing this or any other failure, and changing already-working shipped behavior wasn't part of this task.

**Also transparently: a self-inflicted e2e disruption, corrected before trusting any result.** Stopped the dev server via `preview_stop` while a background full-suite run was still using it (the exact mistake flagged in this session's own Capacitor-wrap entry, repeated once) — 3 of 4 specs failed with `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED`/`ERR_CONNECTION_RESET`, and a second, redundant single-spec run accidentally started on top of it. Neither result was trusted: restarted the server, let both stray runs finish and exit fully, then re-ran the complete suite once, untouched, before drawing any conclusion — which is what surfaced the real `rewash-hard-gate.spec.ts` regression above (the disrupted run's own copy of that spec had failed too, but for the wrong, confusable reason, on a 1.0m run that got far enough to hit the real bug before the connection-refused pattern would have explained it).

**Verification:** `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx oxlint` clean (same 3 pre-existing warnings as every prior entry this session, none introduced here). Java compiles clean against the real jar and Capacitor's plugin framework (`gradlew compileDebugJavaWithJavac`, verified before touching anything else — this is what caught and let me fix a `BluetoothAdapter.getDefaultAdapter()` deprecation note by switching to `BluetoothManager`, the one thing not yet a runtime concern but free to fix in code I'd just written). Unit suite 79/79. Full Playwright suite, clean run: **4/4 passed, including the newly-fixed `rewash-hard-gate.spec.ts`.** `npm install` (`@capacitor/preferences`) added zero new `npm audit` findings. Full debug APK build (`gradlew assembleDebug`, including `npx cap sync android` picking up the plugin and the resized web assets) — see the chat report for the result of this run.

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** Capgo, iOS, any business-logic change, the photo compression issue, the launcher icon.

## 2026-07-25 — Capgo live updates: OTA web-layer pushes without a rebuild

**Landing here now, not out of order by mistake:** this whole entry describes work completed before every entry between here and the end of the file (Capgo was task 2 of this session; the camera-scanner/photo-compression/ombor-printing/menejer-chiqim/camera-overlay entries that already follow were tasks 3-7) — but the branch it was built on (`capgo-live-updates`) was never actually merged to `main`, discovered only when a later Capgo bundle upload failed on a missing `notifyAppReady()` that this same entry already describes adding. Cherry-picked in now, placed at its real chronological position rather than at the file's tail, so the log still reads as an accurate timeline. See the new entry after the existing chain below for the merge-gap diagnosis itself.

**Context:** the Android wrap and P1 printer plugin both need a full rebuild-and-reinstall for any change. Everything else — UI, business logic, report queries — is pure web layer, and shouldn't need that. Capgo pushes web-layer bundles over the air on top of the existing bundled-assets model.

**Inspected and reported the plan before installing, per the task's gate.** Verified the actual package against its real `.d.ts` (npm-packed into a scratch dir and read directly — the first fetched doc page showed a self-hosted-flavored example that didn't match what "use Capgo's hosted service" needed) rather than trust a summarized doc fetch. That surfaced the exact mechanism for every requirement:
- `autoUpdate: 'atBackground'` is a named mode, not something to hand-build: *"Check and download automatically on each foreground check, then apply the update the next time the app moves to background."* That's requirement 3 verbatim — no manual `@capacitor/app` resume-listener needed, it's built into the mode.
- `updateUrl`/`channelUrl`/`statsUrl` default to `https://plugin.capgo.app/...` — Capgo's hosted service is the unset default; setting them is what self-hosting would require. Leaving them out **is** the hosted-service config.
- Rollback (requirement 4) isn't a separate flag — it's automatic whenever `notifyAppReady()` doesn't fire within `appReadyTimeout` (default 10s). The CLI itself also refuses to upload a bundle that doesn't call `notifyAppReady()` in source, unless `--no-code-check` is passed — a second safety net against shipping an unrecoverable bundle.

**Dashboard (user) vs. code (mine), as executed:** user signed up, got an API key, logged in via the CLI, and registered `com.batuexport.app` on Capgo Cloud (org "batuexport app") before this session touched anything. Deliberately did not run the guided `capgo init` wizard even though it exists — its own description says it "adds code for updates," which would have collided with the hand-written wiring below.

**A — package and config, as built:**
- `npm install @capgo/capacitor-updater` (8.51.2, Capacitor-8-compatible). Confirmed **zero new dependencies**: `npm ls @capgo/capacitor-updater` shows it as a bare leaf, and the package-lock diff is 10 lines (the one new entry, nothing else touched) — the `npm audit` count jumping from 3 to 20 findings after this install is unrelated to it (see Verification).
- `capacitor.config.ts`: added a `plugins.CapacitorUpdater: { autoUpdate: 'atBackground' }` block. No `updateUrl` override (hosted service), no `appReadyTimeout` override (10s default is generous for this bundle size — `notifyAppReady()` fires before any data loads, so it's bounded by JS parse/execute time, not network).
- `main.tsx`: `CapacitorUpdater.notifyAppReady()` called immediately after the root render, before `App`/`AuthProvider` ever gets to its Supabase session check — placement matters here, the docs explicitly warn against waiting for any network request first. Safe to call unconditionally on the web build too: the package ships an official `CapacitorUpdaterWeb` no-op implementation (confirmed by reading `web.d.ts`), so this needed no `isNativePlatform()` guard the way the app-specific P1 printer plugin does.
- `npx cap sync android` — published npm plugin, self-registers via the same mechanism as `@capacitor/preferences`; no manual Gradle or `MainActivity.java` change, confirmed by the sync output listing both plugins and a subsequent clean `assembleDebug`.

**B — bundled assets stay the fallback.** Untouched by this task: no `server.url` was ever set (Android wrap entry), and CapacitorUpdater doesn't require one — it patches the existing local `webDir` bundle, it doesn't switch the app to fetching from a remote origin. First launch and any launch with no signal still run whatever's in `dist-native`, OTA or not.

**C — update strategy: `atBackground`**, set explicitly rather than relying on the equivalent default (`true`), so a future reader doesn't have to know that boolean means this specific mode. Checks on every foreground (app start and resume both count), downloads without blocking the UI, applies only when the app next backgrounds or relaunches — never mid-session, so Ombor is never interrupted mid-scan.

**D — rollback.** Enabled by construction, not a setting: as long as `notifyAppReady()` fires (confirmed wired in `main.tsx`), a bundle that fails to boot at all — the only thing this checks — reverts automatically within the 10s default `appReadyTimeout`. `autoDeleteFailed` (default `true`, left untouched) cleans up the failed bundle afterward so it isn't retried.

**E — env vars survive an OTA push, verified directly, not assumed.** Ran `npm run build:native` and grepped the output bundle for the real `VITE_SUPABASE_URL` and `VITE_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` values from `.env` — both inlined, no leftover `import.meta.env` lookup. This is the exact same build command (and the exact same `dist-native` folder Capgo's CLI defaults `--path` to, from `capacitor.config.ts`'s `webDir`) that produces what ships in the APK — there is no separate "OTA build" path that could diverge and omit them. A bundle missing these would produce exactly the "failed to fetch" login failure the task named; this build doesn't.

**Channel setup — blocked by a live Capgo backend inconsistency, not a CLI mistake.** `channel add production com.batuexport.app --default` failed with "App … does not exist." Diagnosed from four independent angles before concluding this wasn't fixable by retrying: `app add` itself reports a duplicate-key error (the app demonstrably exists), `organization list` shows the one org has "Apps: 2," yet `app list`, `channel add`, and `app debug` all fail to find this specific app by id. Every WRITE-shaped path confirms the app exists; every READ-by-id path can't see it — consistent with a backend/caching bug on Capgo's side (their CLI even documents a "Cloudflare rollout decision cache TTL," suggesting a caching layer sits in front of at least some reads), not anything wrong with the app registration itself or with this repo's setup. Not resolved as part of this task — flagged for the user to check via the Capgo dashboard UI directly (which may not share the CLI's specific read path) or retry once resolved. Everything else in this task — the code wiring, the env-var proof, the APK build — is independently complete and doesn't depend on the channel existing yet.

**Release flow (once the channel exists):**
```bash
npm run build:native
npx @capgo/cli@latest bundle upload com.batuexport.app --channel production
```
`--path` isn't needed — the CLI defaults it to `capacitor.config.ts`'s `webDir` (`dist-native`), which is exactly what `build:native` produces. The CLI's own `--no-code-check` bypass exists but is deliberately not part of this flow — its default (checking) is the safety net described under D.

**Adding a staging channel later** (the user's ask, structured for now rather than built now): `npx @capgo/cli@latest channel add staging com.batuexport.app` (no `--default`, so it never becomes what devices get automatically), then a specific test phone can be pointed at it via a Capgo dashboard Device Override — no app rebuild, no code change. This is why `defaultChannel` was deliberately **not** set in `capacitor.config.ts`: hardcoding it there would override the cloud-side channel assignment on every device running this APK, which would make exactly this kind of single-phone staging test impossible without a rebuild. Leaving it unset keeps the channel assignment fully cloud-controlled, which is what "test on one phone before it hits all of them" needs.

**Verification:** `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx oxlint` clean (same 3 pre-existing warnings as every prior entry, none introduced here). `npm audit`'s finding count (3 → 20) is unrelated to this install — confirmed via the package-lock diff (10 lines, all for the new dependency-free package) that nothing else in the tree changed; the newly-surfaced findings (`react-router-dom`, `postcss`, `workbox-build`, `exceljs`'s `archiver` chain) are pre-existing dependencies newly flagged by the advisory database, not introduced here — worth a separate look, not this task's scope. Unit suite 79/79. Full debug APK build succeeded (`gradlew assembleDebug`, `BUILD SUCCESSFUL in 5m 47s`, both `@capacitor/preferences` and `@capgo/capacitor-updater` compiled and packaged in). Live-browser check of the untouched web path: zero console errors, login screen renders normally, confirming `notifyAppReady()`'s web no-op is safe. Full Playwright suite, clean run: **4/4 passed.**

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** native fixes (camera, printer) still need a full rebuild regardless — OTA only ever covers the web layer. iOS, business logic, the Bundle A/B/C fixes themselves.

## 2026-07-25 — Camera scanner in the Android WebView: diagnosis and instrumented fix

**Context:** the BarcodeDetector-plus-ponyfill replacement (earlier DECISIONS entry, "Camera scanner") was already confirmed working on a real phone's *browser*. In the Capacitor Android WebView specifically, the camera opens (permission granted, live video shows) but never decodes — manual entry, untouched, still works. No device is available this session, so this is code/build-artifact investigation only, no runtime device test — that's deferred, see below.

**Investigated and reported before fixing, per the task's gate.** `barcode-detector` was already at `^3.2.1` in `package.json` (confirmed, no install needed — matches what's actually resolved: `zxing-wasm@3.1.1` underneath).

**1 — does `BarcodeDetector` exist in Android System WebView?** Genuinely unverifiable without a device, and said so rather than guessing either way: MDN's browser-compat-data lists `webview_android` as `"mirror"` for the `BarcodeDetector` interface, which is BCD's own shorthand for *"inferred from Chrome for Android's version (83+), not independently verified"* — not a confirmation. Real-world Shape Detection API availability inside a WebView **embedded in a third-party app** (this app's own process, not the standalone Chrome app) has been reported as inconsistent elsewhere, plausibly because the underlying Play Services ML Kit barcode model's on-device availability isn't guaranteed to match the host Chrome app's. `createDetector()` already picks native when present via `'BarcodeDetector' in window`-equivalent (`window.BarcodeDetector ?? PonyfillBarcodeDetector`) — deliberately left this selection logic alone rather than force the ponyfill preemptively on native Android, since that would trade away native's real speed/accuracy advantage on a guess. Instead, added a one-line log (`[BarcodeCameraScanner] detector path -> …`) so the very first device test gives a definitive, permanent answer to this exact question instead of another guess.

**2 — does the ponyfill's wasm actually load, and is it bundled/reachable in `dist-native`?** Checked concretely, not assumed:
- The wasm **is** correctly emitted into `dist-native/assets/zxing_reader-<hash>.wasm` by `build:native`, and **is** correctly copied to `android/app/src/main/assets/public/assets/` by `cap sync android` — confirmed by rebuilding fresh and finding the file in both locations. "Not bundled for the native context" is not what's happening.
- The built JS resolves the wasm via a root-relative URL (`/assets/zxing_reader-<hash>.wasm`, extracted directly from the minified output), which is the same URL shape every other bundled asset (`index.js`, `index.css`) already uses successfully in this app — nothing wasm-specific about the path construction.
- Read the actual installed `zxing-wasm` package source (not just its docs) to check for the well-documented Capacitor class of bug where a WebView serves `.wasm` with the wrong `Content-Type`, breaking `WebAssembly.instantiateStreaming()` (real, longstanding, multiply-reported on `ionic-team/capacitor`'s own issue tracker). `zxing-wasm`'s Emscripten-generated loader already wraps `instantiateStreaming` in a try/catch with a fallback to `fetch().then(r => r.arrayBuffer())` + non-streaming `WebAssembly.instantiate()`, which does not care about `Content-Type` at all — so this specific, well-known Capacitor gotcha should not actually break this library, even if it's present in this environment (it wasn't possible to confirm the WebView's actual header behavior without a device).
- Given this, "the wasm isn't bundled" (the literal claim in the task's own hypothesis) is not what's happening — it's correctly bundled and referenced. What's still open is purely a runtime question — does the `fetch()` actually succeed at that URL inside Capacitor's local WebView server — which needs the device test to answer. Added a log of the resolved wasm URL (`[BarcodeCameraScanner] zxing wasm locateFile -> …`) so a failed fetch shows up as an explicit mismatch against this logged URL rather than nothing at all.

**3 — do camera frames reach the decoder the same way as in the browser?** No evidence either way was found in code — `getUserMedia`'s constraints and the `readyState >= HAVE_CURRENT_DATA` gate are unchanged from the browser-proven version, nothing native-specific there. Added a one-time log of the first ready frame's `readyState`/dimensions so this is confirmed (or ruled out) on the same device test rather than left as a silent assumption.

**The actual code bug, found regardless of which of #1/#2/#3 turns out to be the device's real cause:** the scan loop's `catch {}` around `detector.detect()` was fully silent, on purpose, to treat "no barcode in this frame" as expected per-frame noise (correct — an empty `results` array is the normal no-match case and does not throw). But it also silently swallowed genuine failures — a wasm init rejection, a native API throw — making a **permanently broken decoder indistinguishable from normal scanning** with zero signal anywhere, which is exactly this bug's symptom. This is the one thing fixable with certainty without a device: it was always wrong, independent of which underlying cause turns out to be real.

**Fix (`BarcodeCameraScanner.tsx`), diagnostic-first as instructed:**
- `detector.detect()` failures are now caught, logged once per distinct error message (not every ~100ms, so a persistent failure doesn't flood logcat), and the loop continues exactly as before — no behavior change for the normal "no code in frame" case, only for the previously-invisible real-failure case.
- Three new one-time logs: which detector path was chosen, which wasm URL is in effect, and confirmation the first camera frame reached `HAVE_CURRENT_DATA`. Not temporary debug code to be reverted — this is the requirement 1 deliverable itself, meant to persist and answer the open questions above the next time this runs anywhere, not just once on a phone that's about to get looked at.
- Deliberately did not change `createDetector()`'s native-vs-ponyfill selection, add a runtime native→ponyfill fallback, or touch `vite.config.ts`/`capacitor.config.ts`/`AndroidManifest.xml` — nothing examined there needed changing, and guessing a behavior change on an unconfirmed cause would risk trading away a real capability (native's speed) for no proven benefit.

**Verified live in the browser, not just read:** confirmed via the dev server that both new logs fire correctly (`zxing wasm locateFile -> /node_modules/...` in dev mode, `detector path -> ponyfill (zxing-wasm)` — this sandboxed browser has no native `BarcodeDetector` either, itself a small extra data point that the ponyfill branch is exercised and functional here), the camera-permission-denied fallback message is unchanged, and the manual entry field/submit button are untouched and present.

**Verification:** `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx oxlint` clean (same 3 pre-existing warnings, none new). Unit suite 79/79. Full debug APK build succeeded (`gradlew assembleDebug`, this branch off `main` — `@capacitor/preferences` and the P1 printer plugin compiled and packaged in; Capgo live updates is a separate, not-yet-merged branch and isn't part of this one). Full Playwright suite, clean run: **4/4 passed** (one isolated flake on the first attempt — `chiqim-undo-scan.spec.ts` timed out waiting for a request row unrelated to anything this task touched; reproduced clean in isolation, then clean again across a full second run, so not attributed to this change). **Stopped here, per the task — no device test performed.** Exact phone steps are in the chat report, along with what each of the three new log lines should show and what a passing vs. still-broken result looks like for each.

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** manual entry (already works, untouched), the P1 printer plugin, photo compression, any business-logic change.

## 2026-07-25 — Photo compression retry: robust decode instead of a friendlier failure

**Context:** the decode fallback from the earlier "Qorovul photo upload silent failure" arc (`createImageBitmap` → `<img>`+blob URL) turned a silent, invisible failure into a shown error, but didn't eliminate the underlying failure — certain real-phone photos still needed the user to reselect the same file two or three times before one attempt succeeded. `imageCompress.ts`'s `compressImage()` is shared by every `PhotoField.tsx` consumer (Qorovul's `GateStageForm`, Ombor's `IntakeAcceptForm`, both Laborator Tahlil forms) and Menejer's `KirimForm.tsx` (its own independent call to the same function) — one fix in the shared function reaches all of them.

**Investigated per the task's three candidate causes:**
- **HEIC/format rejection** — unlikely to explain the reported pattern. A format the decoder genuinely can't handle would fail the same way on every retry of the identical file; the reported symptom (succeeds after 2-3 *reselects of the same photo*) is inconsistent with a hard, deterministic per-file rejection.
- **Oversized dimensions exceeding a hard canvas/decoder limit** — same reasoning, would also be deterministic per file, not retry-sensitive.
- **A memory ceiling during full-resolution decode — the one consistent with "succeeds on retry."** Real phone camera captures run 4000-8000px on the longest edge; decoded to raw RGBA pixels before any resizing happens, a single image can be 100+ MB in memory. Both existing decode paths (`createImageBitmap` and `<img>`+`decode()`) decode at full source resolution — the resize to 1600px longest-edge only happens afterward, on canvas. A transient memory-pressure failure that clears by the next attempt matches the reported pattern exactly; a hard rejection does not. No device was available to confirm this directly (reasoned from the code and the reported pattern, as the task allowed), so the fix doesn't bet everything on this one diagnosis — see below.

**Fix (`imageCompress.ts`), addressing all three candidates at once rather than guessing which is real:**
- **Downscale before decode, for the leading (memory) hypothesis.** `decodeViaBitmap` now passes `createImageBitmap` a `resizeHeight` hint for files over 2MB (a zero-cost, size-based proxy for "likely a full-resolution capture," chosen specifically to avoid needing to decode first to check actual pixel dimensions). **Deliberately `resizeHeight` alone, never `resizeWidth` and `resizeHeight` together** — per spec, `createImageBitmap` only preserves aspect ratio when exactly one resize dimension is given; passing both stretches the image to fit that exact box whenever the source ratio doesn't already match, which would ship a visibly distorted audit-trail photo. Height, not width, because these are gate/intake/lab photos taken holding the phone normally (portrait) — height is already the longer edge there, so this directly bounds the true longest edge; for a landscape photo it still shrinks both dimensions together, just not to an exact bound. Gated behind the size threshold so a smaller image is never upscaled by the hint.
- **A third decode path, for the format/mechanism hypothesis.** `decodeViaDataUri` — same `<img>`/`decode()` pipeline as the existing fallback, but loaded via a `FileReader`-produced base64 data: URI instead of a `blob:` URL. A genuinely different loading mechanism, not a repeat of the existing fallback: if a failure is specific to how a given browser/WebView resolves a `blob:` URL rather than the image codec itself, a data: URI sidesteps that by handing the browser the complete file inline.
- **Automatic retry, for the transient-failure hypothesis directly.** All three paths are tried in order; if every path fails, the whole sequence repeats up to 3 times with a short pause between rounds, before finally propagating the error. This targets the *reported symptom* head-on — automates exactly the "reselect the same file two or three times" workaround the user was doing manually, so it resolves itself instead of costing a manual retry each time.
- **Logged, not silent, per path and round** (`console.warn('[imageCompress] <path> failed (round N/3):', err)`) — requirement 1's "add enough logging to identify it." If a real device still hits the fully-unrecoverable case after this fix, the logs show exactly which paths were tried, how many times, and what each threw — not just the final error `PhotoField`'s catch already logs.
- **The final resize/encode step (1600px longest edge, JPEG q=0.7, ~300KB target) is untouched** — same canvas/`toBlob` code as before, unreached by any of the above; requirement 3 is satisfied by not touching it, not by re-verifying it.

**Verified live in a real browser, not just reasoned — specifically the riskiest assumption (does a single-dimension resize hint actually preserve aspect ratio, or could it distort).** Generated real random-noise JPEGs (flat-color synthetic images compress far too efficiently to cross the 2MB threshold and don't exercise this path at all — caught that on the first attempt) at 3000×4000 and 4000×3000 through `createImageBitmap` directly: `resizeHeight: 2400` on the portrait file produced 1801×2400 (expected ratio 0.7500, actual 0.7504 — a 0.05% difference from integer pixel rounding, not distortion); on the landscape file it produced 3200×2400, exactly matching the source's 4:3 ratio. Confirmed the full `compressImage()` pipeline end to end on a normal-size synthetic image too. `PhotoField.tsx` itself is unchanged — same `compressImage()` call signature, same success/error handling — so this integration contract was never at risk.

**Verification:** `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx oxlint` clean (same 3 pre-existing warnings, none new). Unit suite 79/79. Full Playwright suite, final run: **4/4 passed.**

**🚩 Flagging, not fixing (out of scope for this task): `rewash-hard-gate.spec.ts` line 206's soft-warning assertion is now unreliable, confirmed via direct investigation, not assumed.** Across this task's verification runs it failed twice with the identical received value (`"Butun palletlar bilan aniq mos kelmaydi — eng yaqin: 3,510 kg yoki 4,110 kg"` against an expected `/(eng ko'p|eng yaqin): 0 kg/` pattern), including once in complete isolation (`npx playwright test tests/e2e/rewash-hard-gate.spec.ts` alone, no other spec involved) — then passed clean on a later run with no code change in between. Confirmed this has no connection to this task: the spec has zero references to `PhotoField`, `compressImage`, or any photo upload at all. This is the same assertion the "UX pass, prompt 2/2" entry already named as fragile ("its previously-flagged fragile 0kg assertion happened not to trigger this run") — consistent with a feasibility-hint assertion that depends on the *exact* set of available pallet weights for one owner/type/calibre combination in the shared, no-branch, accumulating live test database (CLAUDE.md's own "Plan limitation" testing-workflow section), which drifts over time as more TEST- fixtures are created across sessions. Worth a dedicated look — either seeding a dedicated zero-leftover scenario for this assertion or loosening it to not depend on an exact accumulated total — but that's a test-maintenance task of its own, not something to fold into a photo-compression fix.

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** upload/RLS/storage (already confirmed correct in the earlier arc), business logic, the camera scanner, the P1 printer, `PhotoField.tsx`/`KirimForm.tsx` themselves (both call the same fixed function unchanged).

## 2026-07-25 — Ombor printing gaps: pre-Tugallash reprint, true W2 ordering

**Context:** three named gaps in Ombor's finished-goods printing flow, following on from the P1 printer integration.

**Inspected and reported before coding, per the task's gate — two of three turned out already solved or needing no new data, one turned out to need a small schema addition:**

**Requirement 3 (print button on KIRIM W2) — already done.** The earlier P1 printer task already added `Barcode1Display` per-row in `OmborIntakeTab.tsx`'s "2 · Qabul qilingan" list, unconditionally (not gated by the `defaultOpen` auto-print flag) — it already works for older accepted lines exactly as asked. Confirmed live, nothing to build.

**Requirement 1 (reprint before Tugallash) — confirmed existing data, no query change.** `useMoykaOutput`'s `OutputSerial.pallets` already carries every already-received pallet for an in-progress serial, and `OmborTayyorTab.tsx` already had a `palletList()` helper rendering exactly this with a `Barcode2Display` per pallet — just only reachable from inside the Tugallash-confirm view. Fix is pure presentation: a new `expandedPallets` toggle (mirrors Window 2's own existing `expandedCompleted` pattern) surfaces the same helper independent of that flow.

**Requirement 2 (W2 newest-first) — the real surprise, flagged rather than silently patched.** `completed` was already sorted, but by `lastReceivedDate` (last pallet's receipt date) — a proxy, because `wash_cycles` had no column recording the actual Tugallash moment (confirmed live: `id, serial, cycle_no, status, final_loss_pct, voided_at`, nothing else). That's the real bug: a serial finished today whose last pallet was physically received days earlier sorts by that older date, not today. Applied, flagged rather than silently skipped or silently applied without mention (task's own "Flag if a query change is needed"):
```sql
alter table wash_cycles add column finalized_at timestamptz;
```
Set once, in `handleTugallash`'s existing upsert, at the real finalization moment. Left null for all 20 existing `final` rows (no historical record exists to backfill honestly) — `useMoykaOutput.ts`'s sort key is `finalizedAt ?? lastReceivedDate`, so already-sorted older rows don't regress and every new Tugallash from here on gets the precise signal. No RLS change, fully additive, zero rows affected beyond the new nullable column.

**Verified live against real data, not assumed correct from the diff:**
- Expanded "Qabul qilingan palletlar (N)" on an in-progress serial with 5 existing pallets — all 5 individually reprintable via their own "Barcode #2" button, with zero clicks into Tugallash. Added a 6th pallet through the real form to confirm the count/list update live and coexist correctly with the unchanged `lastBarcode`-driven "just saved" block (both showing, harmless overlap) — then voided that verification pallet (`finished_pallets.status='bekor_qilindi'`) to restore the real serial's prior state.
- **The W2 sort fix needed a real proof, not just a passing glance** — finalizing an actual in-progress serial would have been a real, hard-to-undo change to genuine business data (no "un-finalize" path exists in the app), so built a precise, disposable, TEST-prefixed scenario instead: two synthetic wash cycles with deliberately inverted signals (`TEST-SORT-A`: old last-pallet date, `finalized_at` = now; `TEST-SORT-B`: recent last-pallet date, `finalized_at` = 6 days ago) — exactly the failure shape the old proxy would get backwards. Confirmed live: A ranked above B, correctly. (A red herring along the way: two real rows, `210726-018`/`017`, outranked both — traced to already-known, deliberately future-dated September-2026 fixture data from an earlier session's client-report testing, unrelated to this fix; not a bug.) Cleaned up per CLAUDE.md's own documented convention — voided the pallets and the wash_cycles row (`status='voided'`) rather than deleted; left the TEST-prefixed `kirim_orders`/`kirim_lines`/`moyka_sends` rows in place since deleting them would violate the FK the voided pallet still holds, and they're harmless, clearly-marked fixture data consistent with other TEST- rows already living in this database.
- Confirmed Barcode #1 print button present and functional on KIRIM W2 live (screenshot-equivalent DOM read), including for an older, non-freshly-accepted row.

**Verification:** `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx oxlint` clean (same 3 pre-existing warnings, none new). Unit suite 79/79. Full Playwright suite, final run: **4/4 passed.**

**🚩 Flagging, not fixing (out of scope for this task): `chiqim-undo-scan.spec.ts` failed under full-suite load a third consecutive time across three unrelated tasks, with a different symptom each time** (this run: a full 120s timeout on a CHIQIM-form dropdown at line 61, unrelated to anything Ombor-printing-related), and passed clean in isolation every single time, including this run. Confirmed no connection to this task's changes — the failing step is a `select` in a "Yangi CHIQIM" form, nothing to do with `OmborTayyorTab.tsx`/`useMoykaOutput.ts`. Consistent, recurring, load-only instability across independent changes is itself a signal worth its own investigation (this session already root-caused one real bug behind a similar-looking recurring flake, see the `report-effective-qty-parity.spec.ts` entries) — flagged rather than chased further under this task's own scope.

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** photo compression, camera scanner, any business logic, Tugallash's own behavior (only its upsert payload gained one new field).

## 2026-07-25 — Menejer CHIQIM second window: widened from finished-only to all statuses

**Context:** Menejer's CHIQIM tab second window (`FinishedChiqimList.tsx`) only ever showed `status='olib_ketildi'` requests. Task asked to widen it to show new/pending and in-progress requests too, newest-first, matching the universal second-window pattern — but explicitly gated coding on confirming whether the exclusion was deliberate first.

**Inspected and reported before coding, per the task's gate — the exclusion was deliberate, not an oversight, confirmed from the code itself before asking.** `useFinishedChiqimRequests.ts`'s own header comment already asserted the single-status filter was "complete, not partial." Tracing further back, two separate DECISIONS.md entries confirm it was a considered design choice, not a gap: **2026-07-17 "Step 7 prompt 1"** — *"No requests-list was built for the CHIQIM tab: re-reading §5.4, 'Dashboard Window 1: requests ready to load' belongs to Ombor's own screen, not Menejer's"* — and **2026-07-18 "Step 7 prompt 4"** (closing Step 7) — the finished-only hook was built exactly as designed, same single-equality-filter reasoning. Stopped and reported this history rather than assuming it was safe to widen; user confirmed proceeding after review.

**A second, related finding surfaced during inspection: `order_status` has 4 values, but `chiqim_requests` only ever carries two in practice.** The only status transition ever applied is `kutilmoqda → olib_ketildi` (the stage-2 gate trigger, `complete_chiqim_stage2`, 0020) — `qabul_qilindi`/`yakunlandi` belong to KIRIM's lifecycle and are never written here. So "in-progress" (Ombor actively scanning/loading) isn't a distinct `status` value — that phase lives in `dispatch_manifest`/`ombor_finished_at`, not this column. Reported this rather than silently building a 3-state pill that the schema can't actually produce; proceeded with a 2-state pill (Kutilmoqda/Olib ketildi), matching how `KirimOrdersList.tsx` — Menejer's own sibling second window for KIRIM, using the same enum — already handles it (2 real values mapped, same shape).

**Implementation — `KirimOrdersList.tsx` used as the direct template throughout, not a new pattern invented:**
- `useFinishedChiqimRequests.ts`: dropped `.eq('status', 'olib_ketildi')`. TEST- plate filtering unchanged (still no toggle, per the app's established no-show-test-data-switch precedent).
- Sort key changed from `weighing?.completed_at` (null until Qorovul's stage 2 — would've sunk every pending row to the bottom) to `created_at` (always present), via the shared `sortByDateDesc` utility rather than another hand-rolled `localeCompare` — the same fix shape `useMoykaOutput.ts` already got this session for an analogous null-sort-key problem.
- `FinishedChiqimList.tsx`: added a `STATUS_LABEL` map (`kutilmoqda`→pending/amber, `olib_ketildi`→ok/emerald, same `StatusPill` component), dynamic per-row instead of the old hardcoded "Olib ketildi". Heading renamed "Yakunlangan so'rovlar" (Finished requests) → "Yuborilgan CHIQIM so'rovlari" (Submitted CHIQIM requests) and the empty-state text updated to match — the old heading would have actively misrepresented a pending row's own list as "finished," not just be stale wording. Drill-down/expand needed no changes: every field was already null-safe (`w?.`, `fmt(null)` → '—'), inherited for free from historical rows that already had null `ombor_finished_at` before this feature existed.

**🚩 Real gap found and fixed, not just the literal ask: the second window had no live-refresh wiring at all.** `MenejerChiqimTab.tsx` passed `onSaved={() => {}}` — a no-op — because under the old finished-only filter a freshly saved request could never reach `olib_ketildi` within the same page load anyway (it had to clear the whole gate/scan chain first, on other roles' screens), so the missing refresh trigger was invisible. Widening the filter made this load-bearing: without it, a fresh request would only appear after a full remount, missing the task's own "appears immediately" requirement. Fixed by mirroring `MenejerKirimTab.tsx`/`KirimOrdersList.tsx`'s existing `refreshKey` pattern exactly — a counter in `MenejerChiqimTab.tsx`, bumped in `onSaved`, threaded through `FinishedChiqimList`'s new `refreshKey` prop into the hook's `useEffect` dependency array.

**No view involved, nothing to flag there (task's explicit ask).** `useFinishedChiqimRequests.ts` queries `chiqim_requests` directly, not through a view.

**Testing.** Updated `full-chain.spec.ts`'s own assertions, which previously asserted the *old* behavior directly (a freshly-saved request NOT visible in the finished list) — now asserts the opposite: right after Menejer saves, the request is immediately visible with a "Kutilmoqda" pill, no navigation in between. The later, post-full-chain assertion (completed request shows "Olib ketildi", TEST-prefixed `FILTERED_PLATE` fixture stays hidden regardless of status) is unchanged in intent, just re-pointed at the new heading text — still proving the TEST- filter specifically, not the status filter. `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx oxlint` clean (same 3 pre-existing warnings, none new, none in touched files). Unit suite 79/79. Full Playwright suite: **3/4 passed** — `full-chain.spec.ts` (carrying this task's own new assertions) passed clean.

Re-ran the spec in complete isolation to rule out cross-test pollution from this run specifically — **failed again, identical received value**, confirming this is a deterministic mismatch against the current accumulated live pallet-weight state for that assertion's owner/type/calibre combination, not load-order flakiness this time. ## 2026-07-25 — Camera scanner: aiming frame + on-screen scan feedback restored

**Context:** Android WebView camera scanning shows a full-screen live feed with no overlay — no aiming target, no visible confirmation of a scan's outcome. The browser version (before this session's earlier decoder rewrite) showed both. Decoder/camera-selection itself is confirmed working (separate, prior task) — this is purely the scanning UI.

**Item 1, confirmed via the actual removal commit, not assumed.** `git show 755746c` (this session, "Replace html5-qrcode with BarcodeDetector") shows the old `Html5Qrcode.start()` call passed `qrbox: { width: 280, height: 120 }` — html5-qrcode's own library code drew that as a dimmed-overlay-with-cutout directly into the DOM container it managed. The commit removed html5-qrcode entirely (confirmed necessary at the time: it never decoded a real printed CODE_128 label on hardware) and replaced it with a bare `<video>` element — taking the overlay-drawing with it. Nothing filled the gap; not tied to the WebView specifically, the same commit affects the web build too.

**Item 3, confirmed the feedback data path was never actually broken by that commit — a placement problem, not a missing-code problem.** `processBarcode` (`OmborChiqimTab.tsx`, untouched by 755746c — a different file) still sets `scanError` on every failure reason (duplicate/not-found/not-in-stock-covers-voided/not-lab-passed/claimed/wrong-line) and still updates `scannedByRequest` on success, driving the per-line progress bars and the running pallet count — all fully reactive before this task. The real problem: that feedback renders below the camera view and manual-entry form, off the operator's focus while aiming a phone. Combined with no aiming guide, a scan rarely lands cleanly, so correctly-wired feedback is rarely seen to fire — both symptoms share item 1's root cause.

**Implementation, `BarcodeCameraScanner.tsx`:**
- Video wrapped in a `position: relative` container (same `max-w-xs`/centering it already had, moved from the video element to the wrapper so overlay children align to its exact rendered box regardless of the video's own intrinsic aspect ratio).
- Aiming frame: centered rectangle, same 280:120 ratio as the old `qrbox` — a deliberate callback to the one dimension the previous, real UI already got right, not an arbitrary new proportion. Built with a `shadow-[0_0_0_9999px_rgba(0,0,0,0.35)]` spread-shadow trick (dims everything outside the rectangle) instead of clip-path/SVG — simpler, no extra DOM, well-supported. `pointer-events-none`, purely decorative.
- **Deliberately not cropping frames to that region for the decoder**, despite the task allowing it "if practical" — the native `BarcodeDetector`/ponyfill `detect()` call has no region-of-interest parameter; doing this for real would mean an extra canvas draw every ~100ms in the scan loop, which is the file's own explicitly off-limits territory (decoder logic, confirmed working). Recording the judgment call rather than silently skipping it.
- New `ScanFeedback` prop (`{ tone: 'ok' | 'problem'; message: string }`, exported type) renders as a banner pinned to the bottom of the video. Deliberately NOT reusing `toneStyles`' own bg/text pair for this — those are subtle/light colors meant to sit on a white card, and would be close to illegible overlaid on an arbitrary live camera feed. Uses solid, high-opacity colors instead (`bg-emerald-600/95`/`bg-red-600/95`, white text).

**Implementation, `OmborChiqimTab.tsx`:** new `scanFeedback` state (single flat state, not per-request — same reasoning as the adjacent, pre-existing `scanError`: only one request row is expanded/scannable at a time, per that state's own comment). A `showScanFeedback()` helper sets it and auto-clears after 2.5s (ref-tracked timeout, cleared on unmount too) so it doesn't sit stale once the operator moves on. Called from the exact two outcome points `scanError`/`scannedByRequest` already update inside `processBarcode` — reusing the identical message strings for failures, a new success message (`✓ Qo'shildi — {weight} kg`) that echoes the actual pallet weight just counted. Zero changes to `resolveScan`/any resolution logic — purely an additional presentation call alongside each existing one. The pre-existing below-camera `scanError` note is left exactly as-is (not removed) — a persistent record for anyone glancing back, complementary to the new fleeting on-camera banner, not a duplicate to eliminate.

**Verified live — camera hardware unavailable in this sandboxed Browser pane (confirmed: `getUserMedia` is blocked, same standing limitation as every camera task this session), so verification used a different, still-real method: logged in as `test-ombor`, opened Skladdan CHIQIM, expanded a real in-progress request, clicked "Kamerani yoqish."** The `cameraError` fallback path rendered correctly with zero crash, proving the new overlay markup survives the no-camera case. Then inspected the actual computed DOM/CSS directly (not assumed from the source): wrapper 320×150, aiming-frame box 256×110 — 256/320 = 80% exactly, 256×(120/280) ≈ 110 exactly, confirming the aspect-ratio CSS computes correctly; `box-shadow` and `border` matched the intended mask/border values. Resized to mobile (375×812): frame box 239×103, still 80%-of-wrapper and still aspect-correct — requirement 4 holds at phone width. Feedback banner verified for both outcomes via the manual-entry path (which calls the identical `processBarcode`/`showScanFeedback` as the camera path): an invalid code produced `bannerFound: true, text: "Bunday barcode topilmadi.", bg: red/95%, color: white`; a real, available Isfara/Kalibr-7 pallet (`PLT-210726-011-07-1`, queried live via SQL, verification-only — never finalized, removed via the existing undo button before moving on) produced `text: "✓ Qo'shildi — 2,400 kg", bg: emerald/95%`, and the page's own progress bar/pallet-count/running-total all updated in lockstep (`2,400 / 2,400 ✓`). This is as complete a verification as this environment allows; the real proof is still a phone aimed at a sticker, per the device-test steps below.

**Testing.** `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx oxlint` clean (same 3 pre-existing warnings, none new, none in touched files). Unit suite 79/79. Full Playwright suite: 4/4 passed. (One throwaway run in between failed all 4 on `ERR_CONNECTION_REFUSED` — self-inflicted: stopping the Browser-pane preview server moments before launching Playwright's own `webServer` raced port 5173's release; re-ran clean immediately after with nothing else touching that port, confirming it was never a code issue.)

**Device-test steps:**
1. Build and install the base APK (`npm run cap:sync`, then build/install from Android Studio or `./gradlew installDebug` as usual) — this ships in the same native rebuild, not a Capgo OTA push, since it's JS-only but the task explicitly asked for a device build to confirm, not just a web check.
2. Log in as Ombor, open "Skladdan CHIQIM," expand any request with an open target, tap "Kamerani yoqish."
3. Confirm a dimmed, centered, wide (not square) rectangle appears over the live feed — that's the aiming target.
4. Aim a real pallet's Barcode #2 sticker inside the rectangle. Confirm: a green "✓ Qo'shildi — N kg" banner appears at the bottom of the camera view, the target progress bar and "Skanerlangan palletlar" count update.
5. Scan the same sticker again — confirm a red "Bu pallet allaqachon shu ro'yxatga skanerlangan." banner.
6. Scan a sticker for a type/calibre not on this request — confirm the red "Bu tur/kalibr ushbu so'rovda yo'q." banner.
7. If a voided/dispatched pallet's sticker is available, scan it — confirm the red "...bekor qilingan" banner.
8. Confirm manual entry below the camera still works unchanged as a fallback.

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** the decoder/camera-selection logic (`createDetector`, `getUserMedia` constraints, the scan loop's `detect()` call and debounce — all byte-identical to before), any business logic (`resolveScan`/`chiqimScan.ts` untouched), the P1 printer, photo compression.

## 2026-07-25 — Capgo upload blocker: `notifyAppReady()` wasn't missing from the code, it was missing from `main`

**Context:** `npx @capgo/cli bundle upload` refused with "notifyAppReady() is missing in the build folder." `findstr notifyAppReady src\main.tsx` found nothing — but the "Capgo live updates" entry earlier in this file (chronologically task 2 of this session) describes adding exactly that call. Investigated why a documented, described-as-done change was absent from the actual tree.

**1. Searched the whole `src/` tree — genuinely absent, not misplaced.** Zero matches for `notifyAppReady` anywhere under `src/`, confirming the user's own `findstr` finding rather than assuming it was just in a different file.

**2/3. Root cause, confirmed via git history, not assumed: the whole Capgo integration was never merged to `main` — it wasn't removed by a later merge, it never arrived.** `git log --oneline --all -- src/main.tsx` shows exactly two commits ever touching that file: the original scaffold, and `092ab13` ("Add Capgo live updates for OTA web-layer pushes") — and `git show 092ab13 -- src/main.tsx` confirms that commit genuinely added the exact `notifyAppReady()` call described. But `git merge-base --is-ancestor 092ab13 main` returned false: that commit only ever existed on its own branch, `capgo-live-updates`, which was pushed at the end of task 2 but whose PR was never merged (task 43 in the session's own tracker — "Set up production channel on Capgo" — was left `pending` for a related, separate reason: the Capgo backend's own account inconsistency, see below, blocked the channel-creation step that would have prompted closing the loop). Confirmed the gap was total, not partial: `grep -i capgo package.json` on `main` before this fix found nothing at all — no dependency, no `capacitor.config.ts` plugin block, nothing. Every task since (camera-scanner-webview, photo-compression, ombor-printing-gaps, menejer-chiqim-widen, camera-overlay-feedback) branched from `main` and so never had any of it either.

**Fix: cherry-picked `092ab13` onto a fresh branch off current `main`**, rather than re-deriving the integration from scratch — it was a single, clean, purely-additive commit (7 files, all `+`), already fully designed and verified in task 2 (env-var survival across `build:native`, `autoUpdate: 'atBackground'`, the hosted-service config, the intentionally-unset `defaultChannel`). Applied cleanly with one expected conflict: `docs/DECISIONS.md`, since every task appends to it and this commit's own 40-line entry was written against an earlier point in the file. Resolved by placing the Capgo entry at its real chronological position (right after the P1-printer entry, where `capgo-live-updates` actually branched) rather than at the file's current tail, with a short note explaining why a task-2 entry is landing now, during what's chronologically a much later task — so the log still reads as an accurate timeline instead of one entry appearing mysteriously out of order. `npm install` confirmed `node_modules` was already in sync with the restored `package-lock.json` (0 changes, same 20 pre-existing `npm audit` findings already flagged as unrelated in the original entry).

**4. Confirmed the call survives the real production build, not assumed from source.** `npm run build:native`, then searched the actual minified `dist-native/assets/index-*.js` (not the source) for the string: found exactly once, `...re.notifyAppReady();export{...}` — right after the React root's `render()` call, exactly the intended placement. It survives because minifiers (Vite's default, esbuild) rename local variable/import bindings (`CapacitorUpdater` here became the local alias `re`) but do not mangle property/method names accessed via dot-notation on an external object by default — `notifyAppReady` is such a property, not a renameable local identifier, so there was never a real risk of minification hiding it from Capgo's own source-string check. Answers the task's own alternate hypothesis directly: minification is not why the original check failed; the code simply wasn't in the tree being checked.

**Confirmation the bundle now uploads — partial, and the remaining blocker is a separate, already-known issue, not this one.** `npx @capgo/cli@latest bundle upload com.batuexport.app --channel production` no longer raises the `notifyAppReady()` code-check error at all — it passes straight through that check to the actual upload step, which is itself proof the fix works (that check is what produced the user's original symptom verbatim). It then fails with a different, later-stage error: `Cannot get organization id for app id com.batuexport.app`. Checked further with a read-only command, `npx @capgo/cli@latest app list` → `No apps found` — the exact same write-path-exists/read-path-invisible inconsistency already flagged (unresolved) in the "Capgo live updates" entry above, not a new problem and not something fixable from this codebase. This is Capgo's own account/backend state, dashboard-side per the original task-2 split of responsibilities, not touched further here.

**Verification:** `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx oxlint` clean (same 3 pre-existing warnings, none new). Unit suite 79/79. Full Playwright suite: 4/4 passed.

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** the Capgo backend account/organization issue itself (pre-existing, flagged, needs Capgo-side resolution or their support — not a code fix); any of the camera scanner/photo compression/ombor printing/menejer chiqim work already on `main`, none of which touched any file this fix changed.

## 2026-07-26 — Barcode print legibility: quiet zone, module width, shortened encoded value

**Context:** printed 40×30mm labels don't scan (dense bars, near-zero quiet zone); the same code scans fine off a screen, confirming this is print geometry, not the decoder — real photo evidence provided.

**Inspected and reported before coding, per the task's gate.** Decompiled the real LPAPI jar (`javap` against `LPAPI-2026-01-08-R.jar`, cross-checked against the PDF and demo source — same discipline the original P1 printer task used, since that task already found one real PDF/jar divergence) to get `draw1DBarcode`'s exact signature: `(text, type, x, y, width, height, textHeight)`, `width` documented as "the overall display width of a 1D barcode" — LPAPI auto-derives module width from content length + this target. Current call: `x=2, y=1.5, width=36, height=10, textHeight=2.5` on a 40mm label → **2mm quiet zone per side** (real, not zero, but thin).

**Measured module density with the app's own `jsbarcode` dependency, not estimated.** `PLT-210726-011-07-1` is 19 characters (the task text said 20 — minor miscount) and encodes to **233 CODE_128 modules** → `36/233 ≈ 0.155mm/module` at the current width, well under commonly-cited reliable minimums (~0.25mm+) for phone-camera 1D scanning.

**Minimum safe encodable value — investigated, not assumed.** Traced the sole mint point (`FinishedReceiptForm.tsx:59`, `` `PLT-${serial.serial}-${code}-${existing + 1}` ``, inserted verbatim at `OmborTayyorTab.tsx:158`) and confirmed only the constant `PLT-` prefix is safe to drop from the *encoded* value (19→15 chars, 233→**178 modules**, measured):
- The trailing pallet-sequence digit is unbounded/unpadded (`existing + 1`, no `padStart`) — de-hyphenating it would make pallet #10+ for a serial+calibre ambiguous to parse back.
- Calibre codes aren't uniformly numeric — `useCalibres.ts` documents `code: '04' | '06' | 'KN' …` — a digits-only encoding (the task's own stretch proposal) silently breaks for Konditirskiy.

**The real constraint that shaped the fix: on a fixed 40mm label, growing the quiet zone and thickening bars pull in opposite directions unless content shrinks first** — with 19 chars fixed, there's no room to both widen margins and thicken bars; one comes at the other's expense. This is why the prefix-drop isn't cosmetic — it's what makes the other two levers compatible. Reported this explicitly and got confirmation before implementing, along with two other open questions:
- **Text-suppression risk**, confirmed and flagged rather than assumed: `draw1DBarcode` ties its own auto-rendered readable text to the same `text` argument that drives the bars — there's no separate "encode this, show that" option. Shortening the bars would also shorten LPAPI's own text line unless suppressed. Chose to attempt `textHeight=0` and draw the full value explicitly in its place — confirmed this is genuinely unverified (LPAPI's own docs use `0` for `drawText`'s width/height to mean "auto-size," not "hide," so there's no basis to assume the same convention holds for `textHeight`) rather than presenting it as certain.
- **QR as a follow-up**: assessed, not pursued blind. Even the best safe geometry fix (below) lands around 0.19mm/module — a real, verified improvement but still short of a comfortable phone-camera margin. Confirmed to defer until after this fix gets a real print/scan test, rather than build QR speculatively.

**Implementation, `P1PrinterPlugin.java` (`drawAndCommit`):**
- `barcodeEncoded = barcode.startsWith("PLT-") ? barcode.substring(4) : barcode` — encoding-only, `barcode` itself untouched everywhere else.
- `draw1DBarcode(barcodeEncoded, CODE128, 3, 1.5, 34, 7.5, 0)` — x/width tightened from `2/36` to `3/34`: quiet zone grows 2mm→3mm per side (+50%) while module width still improves to `34/178 ≈ 0.191mm/module` (+24% over today) — both gains land simultaneously specifically because content shrank first. `textHeight=0` attempts auto-text suppression (see risk above).
- `drawText(barcode, 3, 9, 34, 3, 2.3)` — new, explicit — the full `PLT-...` value, in the space LPAPI's own auto-text used to occupy. Every other line (`serial`/`typeLine`/`weightLine`/`clientLine`) untouched, exact same position/size as before.

**Implementation, `OmborChiqimTab.tsx` (`processBarcode`):** one-line normalization right after the empty-input guard — `if (!barcode2.startsWith('PLT-')) barcode2 = \`PLT-${barcode2}\`` — reassigns the parameter itself so every downstream use (both `finished_pallets`/`dispatch_manifest` lookups, `resolveScan`'s own `barcode2` field) picks up the normalized value with no other code change. Backward-compatible by construction: a full-value scan/manual entry (older labels, or the prefix typed by hand) matches as-is since the check is a no-op when the prefix is already present. `barcodeLabel.ts` (web preview) deliberately left untouched — it's explicitly a preview only, already documented as free to drift from native (DECISIONS.md "P1 printer integration" — native prints, web previews), and scanning always reads the real printed sticker's actual encoded bars regardless of which build is doing the scanning, so the shared `processBarcode` normalization is what makes scanning work everywhere, not anything in the preview renderer.

**Verified live — the lookup/normalization specifically, not just reasoned.** Camera hardware unavailable in this sandbox (standing limitation, every camera task this session), so verified via manual entry, which shares the identical `processBarcode` code path as a camera decode. Queried a real, available pallet (`PLT-210726-002-06-2`, Isfara/Kalibr 6), typed the prefix-stripped form (`210726-002-06-2`) into the manual field on an open request wanting a *different* calibre (Isfara/Kalibr 7). Result: **"Bu tur/kalibr ushbu so'rovda yo'q."** — the `no_matching_line` outcome, only reachable if the pallet lookup already succeeded (a failed lookup would have produced "Bunday barcode topilmadi." instead). This conclusively proves the normalization correctly reconstructs the full `barcode2` and finds the real row. No state was written (a rejection is a no-op), so nothing needed cleanup.

**Verification.** `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx oxlint` clean (same 3 pre-existing warnings, none new). `./gradlew compileDebugJavaWithJavac` (`JAVA_HOME` pointed at Android Studio's bundled JBR) — **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**, confirming the 7-argument `draw1DBarcode` call and its parameter types genuinely match the real jar, not just my reading of the docs. Unit suite 79/79. Full Playwright suite: 3/4 passed.

**🚩 Flagging, not fixing (out of scope, confirmed unrelated — third occurrence): `rewash-hard-gate.spec.ts` failed on the exact same pre-existing fragile assertion documented twice already above** (line 206, identical expected pattern, identical received value `"...eng yaqin: 3,510 kg yoki 4,110 kg"`). Grepped the spec for every file this task touched (`P1PrinterPlugin`/`OmborChiqimTab`/`processBarcode`) — zero matches. Given this is now the third unrelated task to hit these exact numbers on this exact line, an isolation re-run wasn't needed to establish the pattern further — it's already well past the threshold where this is clearly a real, standalone issue worth its own fix, not something to keep re-flagging.

**Print-test steps (needs a real device — this is the actual proof):**
1. Build/install the updated APK (`npm run cap:sync`, then the usual Android Studio/`gradlew installDebug` build) — this is native Java, ships in a rebuild, not a Capgo OTA push.
2. Print a fresh Barcode #2 label from Ombor (Tayyor Mahsulot → any pallet's "Chop etish").
3. **Check the text first, it's the one unverified piece:** confirm exactly one "PLT-..." line appears under the bars, not two lines (a short one from LPAPI plus a full one from the new explicit `drawText`). If two lines appear, `textHeight=0` didn't suppress the auto-text — report back with a photo and this needs a different suppression approach.
4. Confirm the quiet zone is visibly wider than before and the bars look less cramped.
5. Scan the printed label with a phone camera in the CHIQIM scan panel. Confirm it decodes and resolves to the correct pallet (matches weight/type/calibre).
6. If it still doesn't scan reliably after this, that's the signal to revisit QR — flagged above as the likely next step, not built speculatively here.

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** `barcodeLabel.ts` (web preview, deliberately — see above), `chiqimScan.ts`/`resolveScan` (no changes — normalization happens before it's ever called), the decoder/camera-selection logic, business logic beyond the one-line lookup normalization required to make the shortened value resolve.

## 2026-07-26 — Barcode format: CODE_128 -> QR

**Context:** the print-legibility fix above (quiet zone + module width) measurably improved density, but real printed 1D labels still failed to scan off paper at 40×30mm — bars too dense for a phone camera to resolve even with the improved numbers. On-screen scanning always worked. QR fixes the root cause (a 2D symbol with error correction fits the same data far more efficiently than 1D bars can) instead of chasing 1D density further. Also asked to restore the scan-frame overlay, "regressed again."

**Frame overlay — investigated fresh, found no regression, same as the last time this was reported.** Read `BarcodeCameraScanner.tsx` directly: byte-identical to the version from the camera-overlay-feedback task, and `git log` confirms zero commits touched that file since — the aiming frame and feedback banner were never lost. What's real and necessary regardless: requirement 4 asks for a square frame for QR (the old 280:120 rectangle was 1D-shaped), so it's being reshaped either way — reported the non-regression plainly rather than silently building "restore" work for a bug that doesn't exist in the code.

**Confirmed before coding, against the real jar, not docs:**
