- `LPAPI.draw2DQRCode` exists with a `(String, double, double, double)` overload — found via `javap` on the **concrete** `LPAPI` class (the field this plugin actually holds), not just the `IAtBitmap` interface it implements, which only exposes an int-typed 4/5-arg pair this plugin never calls. `IAtBitmap`'s `ErrorCorrectionLevel`/`DrawParamName.ERROR_CORRECTION` constants exist but have zero real usage anywhere in the DothanTech demo to confirm the `setDrawParam` calling convention against — left at the library default rather than guess at an unverified native call (same judgment call as `textHeight=0` in the prior task).
- Confirmed the size parameter drives a **square** box, not width+height, by reading both real demo usages in `MainActivity.java` — one subtracts the same `qrcodeWidth` from both the label's width and height when computing x/y, which only makes sense if one parameter drives both dimensions.
- Scanner's format list was `['code_128']` only — added `'qr_code'` (kept `code_128` too, so any already-printed 1D labels still scan during the transition). Confirmed `'qr_code'` is a real, valid literal in the installed `barcode-detector` ponyfill's own type union (`utils.d.ts`), not assumed from the spec name alone.
- Current encoded value, and a drift found while checking it: the prior task made native encode `barcode2` with `PLT-` stripped, and `processBarcode` re-prepend it before lookup — but never touched `barcodeLabel.ts` (web preview), which still encoded the **full** value. Native print and web preview were encoding different strings. This task's own "same value" requirement gave a mandate to fix that now.
- **A third rendering path found that neither this task's own reading list nor the prior task caught:** `Barcode2Display.tsx`/`Barcode1Display.tsx` each have their own direct `JsBarcode(...)` call rendering CODE_128 to an on-screen `<svg>` — completely separate from `barcodeLabel.ts`'s canvas/PNG pipeline (that one only backs the web share/download fallback). This is the actual "on-screen scanning works fine" component from every prior bug report in this arc. Left unfixed, it would have kept showing 1D on-screen after this whole migration. Switched both.

**Implementation:**
- **Native (`P1PrinterPlugin.java`):** `draw2DQRCode(barcodeEncoded, 3, 1.5, 10)` — 10mm square, left-aligned at the old bars' x-position (keeps the rest of the label's own left-aligned convention, unlike the web preview which centers — the two layouts already drift by design, see the file's own long-standing comment; value-parity was the requirement, not pixel-parity). Text lines below reflowed to the QR's new (shorter) vertical footprint. Encoded value still drops `PLT-`, same as the prior fix.
- **Web preview (`barcodeLabel.ts`):** replaced `renderBars`(JsBarcode) with `renderQr`(`qrcode` npm package, `QRCode.toCanvas`), fixed 160px/10mm square — no shrink-to-fit loop needed, unlike 1D: `qrcode` auto-picks whatever version the content needs at a fixed pixel footprint, so a longer value just means smaller modules, never a wider symbol. `renderLabel` split into `encodedValue`/`displayValue` params so Barcode #2 can encode the stripped form while still displaying the full `PLT-...` text, exactly mirroring native. New exported `stripBarcode2Prefix()` helper, shared with `Barcode2Display.tsx`'s own on-screen render so the transform has exactly one definition.
- **On-screen (`Barcode2Display.tsx`/`Barcode1Display.tsx`):** swapped `<svg ref>` + `JsBarcode` for `<canvas ref>` + `QRCode.toCanvas`, same 180px/~11mm size, same encoded-value handling as the print/preview paths (stripped for #2, plain serial for #1 — no prefix to strip there).
- **`jsbarcode` removed from `package.json` entirely** — confirmed via grep it had zero remaining call sites after all three switches, not left as dead weight.
- **Scanner (`BarcodeCameraScanner.tsx`):** `formats: ['code_128', 'qr_code']`; aiming frame `aspect-[280/120]` → `aspect-square`, `w-[80%] max-w-[280px]` → `w-[65%] max-w-[220px]` (a QR target doesn't need as much width as a wide 1D rectangle did).
- **Error correction level 'Q'** (25% recovery) chosen deliberately over the library default 'M' (15%) on every web-side QR (`barcodeLabel.ts`, both Display components) — a warehouse pallet sticker exposed to handling/dust/wear reasonably warrants more tolerance than a phone-screen QR would need, and the short content stays comfortably inside Version 1 either way, so there's no density cost to choosing the higher level. Native left at the library default since `setDrawParam` couldn't be verified against the real jar (see above) — worth revisiting as a small follow-up once someone can confirm that calling convention against a real device.

**Density estimate — computed via the real installed `qrcode` library, not guessed.** `QRCode.create('210726-011-07-1', {errorCorrectionLevel:'Q'})` reports Version 1, 21×21 core modules; +4-module margin each side = 29 modules across. At the native 10mm size: **0.345mm/module**, vs. the prior 1D fix's measured 0.191mm/module — a **1.81x** improvement in raw module pitch, which likely undersells the real scannability gain since a 2D square dot is generally easier for a camera to resolve than a 1D bar-width transition at the same pitch.

**Verified live — real end-to-end round-trip, not just reasoned, using the exact code paths the app itself uses:**
- Generated a QR via the app's own `qrcode`/`QRCode.toCanvas` (same call `barcodeLabel.ts`/both Display components make) for a real-shaped value (`210726-011-07-1`), fed the resulting canvas into a freshly-constructed ponyfill `BarcodeDetector({formats:['code_128','qr_code']})` — the exact config `createDetector()` now uses. Result: `decoded: [{value: "210726-011-07-1", format: "qr_code"}]` — exact match, and correctly identified as QR specifically, not a coincidental match on another format.
- Confirmed `code_128` and `qr_code` are simultaneously supported by the same ponyfill build via `BarcodeDetector.getSupportedFormats()` — both `true`.
- Confirmed the on-screen `Barcode1Display` canvas actually rendered real content (180×180, non-blank pixel data), zero console errors.
- Confirmed the reshaped aiming frame renders as a true square live in the browser: `208×208px`, `aspect-ratio: 1/1` computed style — matches `w-[65%]` of the 320px wrapper exactly.
- Feedback banner (unchanged code, already fully verified in the prior task) reconfirmed indirectly: the below-camera `scanError` note fired correctly on a manual-entry attempt, proving `processBarcode` still runs the identical path a camera decode would trigger.

**Testing.** `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx oxlint` clean (same 3 pre-existing warnings, none new). `./gradlew compileDebugJavaWithJavac` — **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**, confirming `draw2DQRCode(barcodeEncoded, 3, 1.5, 10)` genuinely matches the real jar's overload, not just my reading of `javap`'s output. Unit suite 79/79. Full Playwright suite: **4/4 passed**, including `rewash-hard-gate.spec.ts` clean this run.

**Whether geometry alone would have sufficed vs. needing QR — answered by the user's own real-world print test, not guessed here:** the task's framing already established printed 1D failed even after the density fix, which is why this migration happened at all; nothing in this task re-litigates that finding.

**Print-test steps:**
1. Build/install the updated APK (`npm run cap:sync`, then the usual Android Studio/`gradlew installDebug` build) — native Java, ships in a rebuild, not a Capgo OTA push. (The web-side changes — `barcodeLabel.ts`, both Display components, the scanner — are JS/CSS and would ship via Capgo once that account issue is resolved, but test everything together via a full rebuild this time since native changed too.)
2. Print a fresh Barcode #2 label from Ombor (Tayyor Mahsulot → any pallet's "Chop etish"). Confirm a QR square appears where the bars used to be, with the full `PLT-...` text below it unchanged.
3. Scan the printed QR with a phone camera in the CHIQIM scan panel. Confirm: the aiming guide is now a square, not a wide rectangle; it decodes; it resolves to the correct pallet (matches weight/type/calibre) — this last part proves the "PLT-" strip/re-prepend round-trip works on a real device, not just in this session's live-browser test.
4. Scan an old, already-printed 1D label if one is still on hand — confirm it still decodes too (transition support).
5. Open Barcode #1/#2's on-screen panel without printing — confirm the QR shown on-screen (not the printed one) also scans correctly from the phone.

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** `chiqimScan.ts`/`resolveScan` (no changes), the decode loop / `getUserMedia` constraints / detector construction beyond the formats-list addition, the P1 printer's connection/Bluetooth handling, any business logic beyond the encoded-value plumbing already required by the prior task.

## 2026-07-26 — QR "didn't apply": stale native bundle, not a code gap — no source fix, zero-code entry

**Context:** real hardware confirmed the printer outputs a clean QR, but the on-screen Barcode #2 display still showed 1D and the printed QR failed to scan on both web and native, while 1D-on-screen still scanned. Read as "the QR change applied to print only" — asked to check `barcodeLabel.ts`, `BarcodeCameraScanner.tsx`, and the native path independently before touching anything.

**All three checked directly against `main` (PR #66 confirmed merged, `cac22dc`, nothing landed since) — none were out of sync in source.** `barcodeLabel.ts`: `renderQr`/`QRCode.toCanvas` present, `stripBarcode2Prefix` present, exactly as built. `BarcodeCameraScanner.tsx`: `createDetector()` reads `formats: ['code_128', 'qr_code']`, line 99, unchanged since the prior task. `Barcode2Display.tsx`: `QRCode.toCanvas(barcodeRef.current, stripBarcode2Prefix(data.barcode2), ...)`, also unchanged. Re-read all three fresh rather than trust memory of writing them — this is a real hardware report, not a hypothetical.

**Root cause found, not guessed: the Android WebView's bundled JS was stale, independent of the freshly-compiled Java.** `android/app/src/main/assets/public/assets/` (the directory `npx cap sync android` populates from `dist-native`, and the only JS the WebView ever actually loads) carried a modification timestamp from before this session's QR migration. Confirmed definitively by content, not just timestamp — grepped the actual bundled file for the precise array construction `createDetector()` emits: found the literal `formats:[\`code_128\`]`, a single-element array — the pre-migration config — and zero trace of the `qrcode` package's own markers (`toCanvas`+`errorCorrectionLevel` both absent). This is not a partial migration; it's the entire JS bundle never having been refreshed, while `P1PrinterPlugin.java` — compiled straight from source by Gradle, with no dependency on the `cap sync`-populated asset directory — picked up the native change immediately. Explains every symptom as one mechanism: on-screen display stale (old `JsBarcode` still bundled) and scanner stale (`code_128`-only, can't recognize `qr_code`) are the *same* staleness, not two separate misses — which is also exactly why 1D-on-screen kept scanning: both halves of that pairing were consistently on the old system together.

**Fixed by running the correct build step (`npm run cap:sync`), not by touching any source file — confirmed with the same grep, now clean.** Freshly-synced bundle (`index-CC00n31p.js`, new content hash) now contains `formats:[\`code_128\`,\`qr_code\`]` and both `qrcode`-package markers. `android/app/src/main/assets/public/` is git-ignored (confirmed via `git status` showing nothing there) — this was never a shippable/committable gap, purely a local build-artifact one. Most likely explanation for how the tested APK ended up here: a Java-only rebuild (e.g. `gradlew installDebug` run directly) skips the web-asset copy step entirely if `npm run build:native && npx cap sync android` isn't run first — an easy trap precisely *because* the prior task's own change touched both Java and JS in the same PR, so "I only need to recompile the plugin" is a natural but incomplete assumption. The web deploy (Netlify, `netlify.toml` confirms `npm run build` / publish `dist`) is a separate, independently-stale artifact with the same underlying explanation — a fresh deploy off current `main` was needed and evidently hadn't run; not independently verifiable from this environment, flagged for the user to confirm via Netlify's own deploy log.

**End-to-end value chain confirmed against the actual physical label, not a synthetic one.** Queried `finished_pallets` for the label's real printed value (`PLT-210726-014-KN-1`) — exists, `status='in_stock'`, 100kg. Live round-trip in a real browser: stripped the value the same way `stripBarcode2Prefix`/the native plugin do (`210726-014-KN-1`), encoded it through the app's own `qrcode`/`QRCode.toCanvas`, decoded it through a detector built with the app's own `formats: ['code_128', 'qr_code']`, then applied `processBarcode`'s own re-prepend normalization (confirmed present and unchanged, `OmborChiqimTab.tsx` line 135) — result matched the original label value exactly (`matchesRealLabelValue: true`). This is a direct answer to the task's own ask: once the correct bundle is actually running, this specific printed label decodes and resolves correctly; the round trip was never the problem.

**No commit, no branch, no PR for this entry — genuinely zero source changes.** Every file was already correct; the only action taken was running `npm run cap:sync` locally (gitignored output, nothing to commit) and this investigation. Recording it anyway because the finding is real, reproducible, and will bite the next native-only-seeming change too if undocumented.

**Out of scope, not touched:** no source files (nothing needed changing); Netlify's own deploy configuration/history (outside this environment's visibility — needs the user's own dashboard check).

## 2026-07-26 — QR label layout redesign: bigger QR, less text, calibre abbreviated

**Before, confirmed from source (both `drawAndCommit` in P1PrinterPlugin.java and `renderLabel` in barcodeLabel.ts, shared by Barcode #1 and #2):** QR was 10mm, top-left at (3, 1.5) on the native 40×30mm label — left a 27×10mm dead zone to its right, unused. Below it, 5 stacked lines: full barcode value, parent serial, `type · calibre` (full word, e.g. "Konditirskiy"), weight, company. For Barcode #1, the barcode and serial lines printed the *exact same value* twice (`toPrintOptions` in Barcode1Display.tsx sends `barcode: data.serial, serial: data.serial` — a pre-existing duplicate, not something this pass introduced). No configured QR quiet-zone margin (API default 0); relied entirely on a thin 0.5mm gap to the text below. Web's QR was already centered (unlike native's left-aligned original) at the same 10mm, text centered and auto-shrinking/ellipsizing below it.

**Also found and fixed in passing: a stale comment in barcodeLabel.ts, unrelated to this task but in the exact code being touched.** Lines 27-33 claimed the canvas was 800×480 from a "round(50 × 7.992)" calculation — but `LABEL_MM.w` is 40, not 50, and `NATIVE_W = Math.round(LABEL_MM.w * DOTS_PER_MM)` actually computes 320, not 400 (confirmed by executing the exact expression live: `DOTS_PER_MM=7.992…, NATIVE_W=320, CANVAS_W=640`). The prose was stale from before that constant changed; it never affected behavior since `QR_SIZE_PX=160` (old) was already set correctly against the real 16px/mm ratio, not the wrong comment's implied 20px/mm. Corrected the comment to 320×240/640×480 while already editing this exact block for the QR resize.

**Calibre abbreviation mapping confirmed against the live `calibres` table, not guessed.** `code` is `'01'`–`'08'` / `'KN'`, `label` is `"Kalibr N"` / `"Konditirskiy"` — neither is directly `"K4"`, and only `label` reaches the label composers (`Barcode2LabelData.calibre`/`calibreLabel` param), so `abbreviateCalibre` derives it from the label text: `"Konditirskiy"` → `"KN"`, `/^Kalibr (\d+)$/` → `"K" + digits`, anything else passes through unchanged. Deliberately a pattern match, not a 9-entry lookup table — calibres are Sozlamalar-managed (admin-editable, see `CalibresSection.tsx`/`useCalibres.ts`), so a hardcoded table would silently stop abbreviating any calibre added after Kalibr 8; the regex handles future ones automatically, and the fallback means an unrecognized label never crashes or blanks. Two independent copies exist (TypeScript in barcodeLabel.ts, Java in P1PrinterPlugin.java) — same reason `stripBarcode2Prefix` already has two: native and web can't share code, and both comments cross-reference the other to flag if one drifts.

**Two content questions were real judgment calls, not implementation details — asked before coding rather than guessed:** (1) whether to drop "type" (Tur) from Barcode #2's printed line now that calibre is abbreviated and merged with weight — user chose to **keep it, prefixed** (`typeName · abbrevCalibre · weight kg`), not drop it, so the new merged line carries the same information as before, just abbreviated + consolidated, never less. (2) `Barcode2Display.tsx` has a *third* rendering path beyond the two the task named (native print, `barcodeLabel.ts` preview) — a separate on-screen HTML summary row next to the on-screen QR canvas (line 140, `{serial} · {type} · {calibre} · {weight} kg · {owner}`) — user chose to **update it too**, so all three surfaces agree rather than leaving this one showing stale full-detail content after the sticker itself no longer does.

**New layout: QR 16mm (up from 10mm — +60% linear, +156% area), centered horizontally, dominant.** Native: 3 left-aligned lines below it (was 5) — full barcode value (fallback if scanning fails, font size kept at the original 2.2mm since native `drawText` has no auto-shrink and this line's worst-case length didn't change) · `typeName · abbrevCalibre · weight kg` (font kept at the original typeLine's 2.5mm — abbreviating calibre saves roughly what adding the weight suffix costs, so the safe-length calculus this size was originally tuned against didn't actually change) · company (unchanged). Web mirrors the same 3-line content but keeps its own pre-existing, more robust sizing mechanism (auto-shrink for the barcode line, ellipsize for the small fields) rather than adopting native's fixed-size approach — text switched from centered to left-aligned to match native, per the task's explicit ask that both surfaces read the same way. Serial dropped entirely from printed text on both — it still lives inside the encoded QR value for lookups, completely unchanged (verified: `stripBarcode2Prefix`/`barcodeEncoded` construction untouched in this pass).

**Barcode #1 gets a small, necessary side-effect, not a redesign.** `drawAndCommit` and `renderLabel` are shared between both barcode kinds — giving the QR more room meant Barcode #1's original 5 lines (2 of them the exact-duplicate noted above) no longer fit comfortably. Merged its `type` and `weight` onto one line (`typeName · weight kg`), mirroring the exact same consolidation Barcode #2's redesign already required, and dropped the duplicate serial line — the same fix, applied for free. Web's Barcode #1 additionally keeps its pre-existing `date` field (never printed natively — a web-preview-only field per Barcode1Display.tsx's own comment), now 3 fields instead of 4. Nothing about Barcode #1's actual *content* was asked for or added beyond what sharing the layout code with Barcode #2 required.

**Verified, not assumed — real data throughout.** `tsc --noEmit`, `oxlint`, `gradlew compileDebugJavaWithJavac` (via Android Studio's bundled JBR as `JAVA_HOME`, no system JDK in this environment) and all 79 existing unit tests: clean. Live in a real browser: rendered actual label PNGs for a real Konditirskiy pallet (`PLT-210726-014-KN-1`, → "Subxon · KN · 100 kg") and a real numbered pallet (`PLT-210726-001-06-1`, → "Subxon · K6 · 1,800 kg") queried from `finished_pallets`/`calibres` directly — visually confirmed the new layout (bigger centered QR, left-aligned 2-line text) by reading the generated PNGs as images, not just trusting the code. Round-trip scanned the QR *embedded in the actual composited label PNG* (not an isolated QR canvas) through the app's own `BarcodeDetector` construction for both pallets — both decoded and re-prepended back to their exact original `barcode2` values. Logged into the real running app (test credentials from `.env.test`, `TEST_OMBOR_*`) and confirmed the on-screen summary row for a live pallet reads exactly as designed: "Subxon · K1 · 1 kg · Boysun Quritilgan Mevalar" for `PLT-230726-045-01-1`.

**Print-test steps (native — the only thing not verifiable from this environment):**
1. Build via the full sequence, not Java alone: `npm run cap:sync` then the usual Android Studio/`gradlew installDebug` build (see the entry above this one for why skipping `cap:sync` silently ships a stale bundle).
2. Print a fresh Barcode #2 label from Ombor (Tayyor Mahsulot → any pallet's "Chop etish"). Confirm: the QR is now noticeably larger and dominates the top of the label; below it, left-aligned, 3 lines — the full `PLT-...` id, `Tur · Kalibr · vazn kg` (e.g. "Subxon · K4 · 680 kg" — abbreviated, not the full calibre word), then company name. No parent-serial line anywhere on the printed sticker.
3. Scan the printed QR with a phone camera. Confirm it still decodes at the larger size (bigger should only ever help, never hurt) and resolves to the correct pallet.
4. Print one Konditirskiy pallet and one numbered-calibre pallet specifically — confirm `KN` and `K1`–`K8` both render correctly, not just one family.
5. Print a Barcode #1 (raw material) label too — confirm it now shows serial, `Tur · vazn kg` merged, company (3 lines, not the old 5, and no more duplicate serial line) — this wasn't the task's target but shares the same draw code, so it's worth a quick look.
6. Open Barcode #2's on-screen panel without printing — confirm the on-screen QR is also bigger, and the summary line below it reads `Tur · Kalibr · vazn kg · Egasi` (abbreviated calibre, no serial).

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** the encoded QR value (unchanged — only the printed/displayed text and QR size/position changed), the scanner/decode path, `chiqimScan.ts`/`resolveScan`, any business logic, Capgo.

## 2026-07-27 — CHIQIM Option B: pallet-level reservation

**Context:** Option A (2026-07-24, "CHIQIM inline pallet picker") was explicitly non-reserving — the picker helped Menejer calculate a kg target by clicking real pallets, but `chiqim_lines` stored only the number, and any matching pallet at scan time would do. This closes the deferred gap: requests now target specific pallets, Ombor gets a concrete prepare-list, and reservation prevents double-claiming.

**Inspected and reported before coding, per the task's gate — three real gaps found, all confirmed live, not assumed:**
- `chiqim_lines` had no pallet-reference column of any kind (`id, request_id, type_id, calibre_id, qty_kg` only, confirmed via `information_schema.columns`).
- `chiqim_requests` had **no void mechanism and no Menejer UPDATE policy at all** (`menejer_writes` INSERT, `read_all` SELECT, `ombor_updates` UPDATE — confirmed via `pg_policies`). Requirement 4's "freed on request completion or void" and the task's own testing script ("void the request") aren't buildable without first closing this — flagged explicitly rather than building around it, per the task's own "flag any conflict with the append-only/void rules" instruction. Proposed a `voided_at`/`voided_by` column pair (mirrors `wash_cycles.voided_at`, not a new `order_status` enum value — that enum is shared with `kirim_orders`, out of scope to touch) plus a Menejer UPDATE policy scoped to `ombor_finished_at IS NULL` (voiding a request already mid-scan needs a real `dispatch_manifest` unwind, a separate, bigger concern).
- `resolveScan`'s existing type+calibre "largest remaining gap" heuristic (for two lines sharing the same type+calibre) becomes unnecessary once every pallet is reserved to a specific line — confirmed by reading the current logic, not assumed obsolete.

Presented the full schema (below) and three judgment calls in chat, waited for confirmation before writing any SQL: (1) add the void mechanism as proposed, (2) disable manual qty-typing once matching pallets exist — picker becomes the only path, manual typing stays only for the pre-existing zero-pallets fallback, (3) retire the old gap-heuristic in favor of direct reservation lookup. All three confirmed.

**Schema (`0033_chiqim_pallet_reservations.sql`), applied live and as a migration file:**
```sql
alter table chiqim_requests add column voided_at timestamptz;
alter table chiqim_requests add column voided_by uuid references profiles(id);

create policy menejer_voids on chiqim_requests for update
  using (my_role() = 'menejer' and ombor_finished_at is null)
  with check (my_role() = 'menejer' and ombor_finished_at is null);

create table chiqim_line_pallets (
  id uuid primary key default gen_random_uuid(),
  line_id uuid not null references chiqim_lines(id) on delete cascade,
  barcode2 text not null references finished_pallets(barcode2),
  created_at timestamptz not null default now(),
  released_at timestamptz
);
create index on chiqim_line_pallets(barcode2) where released_at is null;
create index on chiqim_line_pallets(line_id);
create unique index chiqim_line_pallets_active_barcode
  on chiqim_line_pallets(barcode2) where released_at is null;

alter table chiqim_line_pallets enable row level security;
create policy read_all on chiqim_line_pallets for select using (auth.uid() is not null);
create policy menejer_writes on chiqim_line_pallets for insert with check (my_role() = 'menejer');

create or replace function release_chiqim_reservations() returns trigger
language plpgsql security definer set search_path = public as $$
begin
  if (new.voided_at is not null and old.voided_at is null)
     or (new.ombor_finished_at is not null and old.ombor_finished_at is null) then
    update chiqim_line_pallets set released_at = now()
    where released_at is null and line_id in (select id from chiqim_lines where request_id = new.id);
  end if;
  return new;
end;
$$;
create trigger chiqim_requests_release_reservations
  after update on chiqim_requests for each row execute function release_chiqim_reservations();
```
`barcode2 references finished_pallets(barcode2)` is valid — `barcode2` IS `finished_pallets`' own primary key (confirmed via `pg_constraint`, same FK shape `dispatch_manifest.barcode2` already uses). `released_at is null` is "currently reserved," derived and enforced by the trigger — never written by client code, same pattern `complete_chiqim_stage2()` already established. The partial unique index is the actual double-claim guard, same strength as `dispatch_manifest.barcode2 UNIQUE` for the existing scan-claim mechanism, just releasable (append-only: released rows stay for history, never deleted).

**Exactly when reservation locks and releases:**
- **Locks** at request creation, in the same `handleSubmit` that already inserts `chiqim_lines` — sequentially, not batched, specifically so each row's own DB-returned `id` correctly ties its own picker selections to the right line (relying on batch-insert `RETURNING` order matching input order wasn't worth trusting for a mapping this load-bearing — a mismatch would silently reserve pallets under the wrong line).
- **Releases** via the trigger the instant `voided_at` OR `ombor_finished_at` transitions to non-null — completion releases too, not just voiding, because `dispatch_manifest` has already taken over as the real claim by then; an unscanned reserved pallet (a shortfall line) shouldn't stay locked forever just because the request finished anyway (§5.4's "never blocks").

**Implementation:**
- **`chiqimScan.ts`** — `resolveScan` no longer takes `lines`/`scannedTotalsByLineId` for matching; takes `reservedLineIdByBarcode: Record<string, string>` instead, built by the caller from data already loaded (no extra query). `no_matching_line` replaced by `not_reserved` — a valid, in-stock, lab-passed, unclaimed pallet is now rejected outright if it isn't reserved for this request, even if its type/calibre would otherwise match a line. `chiqimScan.test.ts` rewritten to match: the old "duplicate type+calibre lines, largest gap wins" test now proves the opposite — reservation resolves the line directly regardless of either line's gap.
- **`ChiqimForm.tsx`** — the qty `TextInput` is `readOnly` whenever `matchingPallets(row).length > 0`; manual typing survives only as the pre-existing zero-pallets fallback. Submit validates every row with real stock actually has a selection (a stock-existed-when-typed-then-got-claimed race, not the normal path). `handleSubmit` inserts lines sequentially, then batch-inserts `chiqim_line_pallets`, with a `23505` (active-reservation race) caught and given the same friendly-message treatment `OmborChiqimTab.tsx`'s own `dispatch_manifest` 23505 handling already uses. Requirement 5 (interchangeable stock, "no special-casing") needed no separate implementation — it falls directly out of the picker becoming mandatory: Menejer clicking "any 3 of 16" already produces 3 named pallets, the same interaction as always, just now persisted.
- **`useAvailableFinishedStock.ts`** — new shared `useReservedPalletBarcodes.ts` hook (`chiqim_line_pallets` where `released_at is null`), excluded here alongside the existing `dispatch_manifest`/lab-status filters, and reused by `ChiqimForm.tsx`'s own `matchingPallets()` so the feasibility hint and the picker can never disagree about what's reserved — same "one derived truth" reasoning `currentCycleLabStatus` already established.
- **`useOmborChiqimRequests.ts`** — query now nests `chiqim_lines(...).chiqim_line_pallets(barcode2, finished_pallets(serial, weight_kg))`, three levels, the prepare-list's actual data. Also added `.is('voided_at', null)` — voided requests never reach Ombor at all (voiding is only allowed pre-scan, so Ombor never had a reason to see one).
- **`OmborChiqimTab.tsx`** — new "Yig'ish kerak — palletlar" section, shown immediately on expand (right after target progress, before the scan zone — the task's own "not a hidden detail," not buried behind a second toggle), one sub-list per line, already-scanned pallets get a checkmark/strikethrough so it reads as a live "what's left" list. `processBarcode` derives `reservedLineIdByBarcode` from `request.lines` (already loaded, no extra query) and passes it to `resolveScan`; the `finished_pallets` select dropped `type_id`/`calibre_id` (no longer needed once reservation replaced type/calibre matching).
- **`useFinishedChiqimRequests.ts`/`FinishedChiqimList.tsx`** — void action, scoped to exactly what `menejer_voids`' RLS allows (`!request.voided_at && !request.ombor_finished_at`), same two-step confirm shape as `OmborChiqimTab.tsx`'s own `Yuklashni yakunlash` (not a browser `confirm()`). A voided request stays visible with a new "Bekor qilindi" pill — this app's void-don't-delete convention, unlike Ombor's window, which excludes voided requests outright.

**Verified live, real end-to-end, exactly the task's own testing script:**
- Reserved two real pallets (`PLT-210726-009-04-1` 1,000kg + `PLT-210726-009-04-3` 100kg, Samarqand Meva Kompaniyasi/Subxon/Kalibr 4) via the picker — qty auto-filled `1100`, field confirmed `readOnly: true`. Saved successfully, appeared immediately in Menejer's own list as `Kutilmoqda`.
- Logged in as Ombor: the prepare-list showed exactly those 2 pallets (barcode/serial/weight), nothing else.
- Manual-entry scanned one listed pallet → accepted, progress bar `100/1,100`, prepare-list marked it `✓` struck-through. Scanned a different, genuinely valid (in-stock, unclaimed, lab-passed) pallet not on the list → rejected with `"Bu pallet bu so'rovga tegishli emas."` — the exact message requested.
- Logged back in as Menejer, re-opened the picker for the same owner/type/calibre → **zero** pallets offered (`"Bu buyurtmachida ushbu tur/kalibrda mavjud pallet yo'q."`), confirming both reservations correctly excluded a second request's availability while the first stayed open.
- Voided the first request (two-step confirm) → pill flipped to `Bekor qilindi`. Reloaded and reopened the picker for the same combination → **both pallets available again**, confirming the release trigger fired correctly on `voided_at`.

**A real e2e consequence, expected once manual typing became conditional — but chasing it down surfaced two genuine bugs, not just fixture churn.** Three specs filled the CHIQIM qty input directly (`chiqim-undo-scan.spec.ts` ×2, `full-chain.spec.ts` ×1) for scenarios where real matching stock exists at that point, making the field `readOnly` and `.fill()` throw. The obvious fix — click the picker instead — immediately surfaced something worse:

**🚩 Real bug #1, found live, not hypothetical: a line with zero reservations became permanently unscannable.** `full-chain.spec.ts`'s own CHIQIM-side KIRIM order used `uniqueTestId(...)` (`TEST-`-prefixed) — and `stock_on_hand_rows` (the picker's own data source) turned out to carry a **third** filter excluding any pallet whose parent `kirim_orders.plate` starts with `TEST-`, the same family as `useFinishedChiqimRequests.ts`/`reportQuery.ts`'s `isTestPlate()` (see `fixtures.ts`'s own `uniqueRealLookingPlate` doc) but never previously documented for this view. So the picker offered *nothing* for the fixture's own freshly-produced pallet, my "click the only PLT- button" locator matched a *different*, unrelated already-visible pallet instead, and the target silently became wrong. Root-caused via the view's actual `pg_get_viewdef` output, not guessed. Fixed at the fixture level: `seedDispatchablePallets`'s plate and `full-chain.spec.ts`'s `PLATE_IN` both switched to `uniqueRealLookingPlate()` (zero other assertions depended on either being `TEST-`-prefixed, checked directly before changing). Picker clicks also switched from a generic "any PLT- button" match to the test's own already-known barcode variable, so a genuine accumulated/unrelated pallet can never be clicked by mistake again.

**🚩 Real bug #2, more fundamental — found via `rewash-hard-gate.spec.ts`'s own scenario, not invented to make a test pass.** That test scans a cycle-2 pallet — produced *after* the request existed — into a request whose line had zero reservations at creation time (the gated cycle-1 stock was correctly invisible). Under the original "reservation is unconditionally authoritative" design, that line could *never* accept anything, ever — not just in this test, but for any real request created before its target stock existed (ordering ahead of production is exactly what the zero-pallets manual-typing fallback is *for*). Fixed in `chiqimScan.ts`: `resolveScan` now checks a pallet's own reservation first (authoritative when present), and only for a line with **zero reservations at all** falls back to Option A's original type/calibre matching (largest remaining gap) — a line that has *any* reservation never falls back, even for an unreserved-but-matching pallet, keeping requirement 3's exclusivity intact. `chiqimScan.test.ts` gained dedicated coverage for both the fallback itself and its interaction with a sibling line that *does* have a reservation. `OmborChiqimTab.tsx`'s `processBarcode` restored `type_id`/`calibre_id` to the pallet lookup and passes `lines`/`scannedTotalsByLineId` back through, both needed by the fallback.

With that fixed, `rewash-hard-gate.spec.ts` itself needed two further changes: its own KIRIM plate switched to `uniqueRealLookingPlate()` (so cycle 2's pallet would be picker-visible), and its "confirm dispatchable" step was redesigned to build a **fresh** request through the normal reserve-then-scan flow rather than reusing the earlier gate-check request via the fallback — a more faithful exercise of Option B's primary path, and independent of whichever request the fallback-based analysis above concerned. Its Direction-1 assertion (`/eng yaqin: 0 kg/`) was also replaced — not patched — with a direct check that the specific gated barcode is never offered by the picker: the old text assertion was fragile against this shared e2e database's own accumulated, unrelated stock (confirmed live: a real 600kg pallet from earlier in this session legitimately makes `nearestBelow` nonzero, which is `checkFeasibility` working correctly against real data, not a bug) — this is the same class of drift already flagged 3× for this exact file's line 206 assertion in earlier entries, now actually resolved for this specific check rather than re-flagged a 4th time.

**Verification.** `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx oxlint` clean (same 3 pre-existing warnings, none new). Unit suite 82/82 (net +3: 2 removed for the retired gap-heuristic, 5 added covering reservation-authoritative matching, the zero-reservation fallback, and their interaction). Full Playwright suite, final run: **4/4 passed**, including `rewash-hard-gate.spec.ts` clean.

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** opening-stock seeding, the cut line, moisture-based selection, the raw/KIRIM side, `chiqimFeasibility.ts`'s own subset-sum logic (still soft-warning only, unchanged).

## 2026-07-27 — Operational data wipe for a clean testing slate

Requested directly (not code-driven): clear every operational row so the app is a fresh slate for end-to-end testing, keeping master data intact. `supabase/seed/wipe-all-operational-data.sql` (2026-07-21) was the obvious starting point but turned out stale in two ways once checked against the live schema rather than trusted as-is: it has no statement for `chiqim_line_pallets` (didn't exist yet when it was written — this session's own CHIQIM Option B table, see above), and it deletes `owners`, which this task explicitly wants treated as master/config data, not operational. Delete order was re-derived from the **live** FK graph (`list_tables` verbose, not the migration files — those turned out stale too: `lab_results.request_id` doesn't exist anymore, it's `parent_serial`/`wash_cycle_id` since the v1.9 reshape).

**Order used** (children before parents, verified against every live FK, not assumed): `chiqim_line_pallets` → `dispatch_manifest` → `chiqim_lines` → `gate_weighings` → `chiqim_requests` → `lab_results` → `finished_pallets` → `wash_cycles` → `moyka_sends` → `storage_intake` → `kirim_lines` → `kirim_orders` → `notes` → `serial_counter` → `audit_log`. `serial_counter` included deliberately — leaving a day's counter row behind would make the next serial minted that day continue from the old count instead of starting fresh at `-001`. Run via the Supabase MCP's direct Postgres connection (same path `apply_migration` uses), not the app's RLS-constrained client — most of these tables have zero delete policies for any role by design (§2.15 "never delete, only void" — see `teardown.ts`'s own comment), so this was the only path capable of clearing them at all.

**Master data confirmed untouched:** `profiles` (10), `settings_limits` (9), `calibres` (9), `product_types` (5), `owners` (6), `product_categories` (1). One judgment call flagged and left as-is per the user's own choice: `owners` contains one inactive row, `"Sinov Mijozi QA"` ("Test Client QA") — textbook test debris but not literally `TEST-`-prefixed, and `owners` is explicitly master data / out of scope for this task, so it was surfaced rather than deleted unilaterally.

**Result, verified live:** all 15 operational tables (461 `audit_log`, 58 `finished_pallets`, 38 `lab_results`, 32 `gate_weighings`, 28 `moyka_sends`, 24 `wash_cycles`, 23 `kirim_lines`, 21 `storage_intake`, 21 `kirim_orders`, 15 `dispatch_manifest`, 14 `chiqim_requests`, 7 `chiqim_lines`, 7 `serial_counter`, 2 `chiqim_line_pallets`, 0 `notes`) read **0** after. No app code, schema, or master data touched.

**Flagged, not fixed here (out of scope for this task):** `supabase/seed/wipe-all-operational-data.sql` itself is now stale for the reasons above — running it as-is today would error on the missing `chiqim_line_pallets` statement and would delete `owners` against this task's own policy. Left as found; worth a follow-up pass whenever it's next needed.

## 2026-07-28 — KIRIM box mass in the net-weight formula

§2.16's net formula was product + boxes, not true product: `effective_qty` (single-line, final) read straight off `gate_weighings.net_kg` (`gruzheny_kg − pustoy_kg`), which is whatever the scale weighed — boxes included, since the gate weighs the whole physical load at once. Real formula: `net product = gate loaded − gate empty − Σ(box mass across the truck's lines)`. KIRIM only, by design — CHIQIM ships finished pallets whose weight is already atomic and fixed (§5.4); there is no box-mass concept on the dispatch side, and nothing here touches `chiqim_requests`, CHIQIM gate stage 2, or dispatch net.

**Where box mass lives.** `storage_intake.box_mass_kg`, mandatory, `check (box_mass_kg >= 0)` — `storage_intake` already *is* "the intake/line record" (PK = serial), and box mass is entered by Ombor at the same accept-time moment as `actual_qty`, so "line accepted" and "line's box mass known" are the same event. Table was empty at migration time (this session's own clean-slate wipe, above), so `NOT NULL` needed no backfill.

**`gate_weighings.net_kg` is untouched** — still the generated `gruzheny_kg − pustoy_kg`, still what Qorovul's own gate screens (`GateStageForm.tsx`, `useGateHistory.ts`, `useKirimTrips.ts`) show, still directly evidenced by the two mandatory scale photos. Box mass is subtracted one layer up, only in the weight-authority derivation, producing a **true net** = `gate_weighings.net_kg − Σ box_mass_kg` that replaces raw `net_kg` everywhere it was being read as the truck's product total: the single-line final `effective_qty`, `truckVariance` (declared vs. gate — was silently biased by the box weight every time, since `declared_qty` is a product-only client claim and raw `net_kg` isn't), the multi-line `lineReconciliation` (sum-of-lines vs. truck total — same bias), and `provisionalVarianceFlag`. No double-counting: `declared_qty`/`actual_qty` were never box-inclusive to begin with (Ombor splits/estimates, doesn't weigh), so box mass is subtracted exactly once, only where raw `net_kg` is read as a truck product total. Multi-line per-line `effective_qty` stays `storage_intake.actual_qty` — unchanged §2.16.1 rule, box mass never leaks into it, only corrects the truck total it's reconciled against.

**Two independent pending inputs.** `deriveEffectiveQty` (`weightAuthority.ts`) gains `totalBoxMassKg: number | null` — `null` until *every* line on the order has been accepted (mandatory field makes "accepted" and "this line's box mass known" the same event; the order-level total isn't complete until every sibling line clears that bar too). Finalization now requires `gateStage2Done && totalBoxMassKg !== null`; either can arrive first. Result gains `pendingOn: { gate, boxMass }`, surfaced through a new shared `pendingLabel()` helper (same file) so `OmborIntakeTab.tsx` and `KirimOrdersList.tsx` — the two live screens reading the TS layer directly — show "tarozi kutilmoqda", "quti massasi kutilmoqda", or both, instead of the old one-size-fits-all text. The six reporting-engine-driven screens (`SerialPassportModal`, `ReportTableRow`, `ReportRowCard`, `KirimRowDetail`, `reportExport.ts`, `OmborMoykaTab.tsx`) deliberately keep the coarser "provisional" text — extending them means growing `report_kirim_rows`' column shape for a UI nuance, scoped out and flagged to the user before coding rather than done silently.

**The SQL twin.** `report_kirim_rows` (a Postgres view) is a second, independent implementation of the same `effective_qty` rule — see `report-effective-qty-parity.spec.ts`'s own header, which exists specifically to catch the two drifting. Migration `0034_kirim_box_mass.sql` adds a `box_mass` CTE (`bool_and(si.serial is not null)` gates the sum to `null` unless every line is accepted, mirroring the TS side's own aggregation) and threads `gw.net_kg - bm.total_box_mass_kg` through every branch that used to read raw `gw.net_kg`. `stock_on_hand_rows`' raw-stock figure reads `report_kirim_rows.qty_kg` directly, so it inherits the fix with no migration of its own.

**Parity test extended, not just patched.** `report-effective-qty-parity.spec.ts`'s `seedOrder` fixture inserted `storage_intake` without `box_mass_kg` — would have broken outright once the column went `NOT NULL`. Every scenario's seed now carries a `boxMassKg`, and scenario E's is deliberately nonzero (a zero box mass couldn't have caught a dropped subtraction). Added **Scenario F**, the one branch that didn't exist before box mass: a multi-line truck where gate stage 2 completes before every line is accepted — the accepted line must show provisional/pending-on-box-mass (not pending-on-gate), the unaccepted sibling stays `declared_pre_intake` regardless. `pendingOn` itself isn't asserted in this test — `report_kirim_rows` doesn't expose that granularity, only `qty_kg`/`provisional` do, so there's nothing on the SQL side for it to drift against; the `pendingOn` combinations (including "both pending," which has no natural single-scenario home in the parity test) are unit-tested directly in `weightAuthority.test.ts` instead.

**Verified live, real end-to-end**, not just via the suite: created a real single-line KIRIM order (1,000 kg declared) through the actual UI, seeded gate stage 1 (1,150 kg) and, once the mandatory box-mass field's presence was confirmed on `IntakeAcceptForm.tsx` (submission with it empty was silently blocked — no `storage_intake` row inserted, confirming "cannot be completed without it"), seeded intake (1,000 kg, box mass 55 kg) and gate stage 2 (100 kg empty) directly via SQL — the same seed-via-SQL pattern the e2e suite itself uses, since this browser environment has no file-upload capability for the two mandatory scale/pile photos. Result, cross-checked in three independent places: Ombor's own list (`Netto 995 kg`), Menejer's `KirimOrdersList` (`995 kg`, truck variance `-5 kg / -0.5%`), and `report_kirim_rows` directly via SQL (`qty_kg: 995`, `truck_variance_diff_kg: -5`) — all agree, and match `1150 − 100 − 55 = 995` by hand. Before this fix, the same inputs would have shown `+50 kg / +5.0%` (raw `net_kg` 1050 vs. declared 1000) — a spurious over-delivery reading caused entirely by the unsubtracted box weight, not an actual commercial variance. Test order deleted after verification (not `TEST-`-prefixed — a real-looking plate, per this suite's own `uniqueRealLookingPlate()` convention — so it needed manual cleanup rather than relying on any `TEST-` filter).

**Verification.** `npx tsc -b --noEmit` and `npx oxlint` clean (same 3 pre-existing warnings, none new). Unit suite 84/84 (net +2: two new `deriveEffectiveQty` cases for the box-mass-only-pending and both-pending branches). Full Playwright suite run once; see test output for final pass/fail.

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** the cut line, opening-stock seeding, any gate-side input beyond reading the already-existing empty-truck weight — `GateStageForm.tsx`, the gate write path, and Qorovul's own screens are unchanged.

## 2026-07-28 — Lab moves inside Moyka, wash-cycle concept removed

**Context:** The product's real physical process has never had a re-wash *cycle* in the sense the schema modeled — SPEC's own wording (confirmed against the mockups before any code changed) is that Laborator's CHIQIM check happens once material is *in* Moyka, not after a completed processing run, and a reject just means the same batch gets re-tested, not that a second, numbered wash cycle begins. The old model (`wash_cycles` one row per cycle, `cycle_no` incrementing, Ombor's own void+resend UI to open cycle 2) was a mechanism invented to represent something that was never actually asked for, and it was the direct cause of a whole class of complexity this session removed: `activeCycles.ts`, `rewash.ts`, `usePendingRewash.ts`, the "sikl N" badges throughout Kirim/Chiqim/reports, `crossPeriodRewash` in the client report, the `qayta_yuvish_pending` WIP bucket, and — most importantly — the fact that the hard gate on Barcode #2 assignment lived at the *wrong* point (dispatch, not packing), meaning an untested serial could physically receive a pallet barcode before Laborator ever saw it.

**Decision (confirmed by the user before any code changed):**
- Keep `wash_cycles` as a real table (do not drop it) — too many FK/RLS/query references for the gain, and it still earns its keep as the one-row-per-serial home for `status`/`final_loss_pct`/`finalized_at` (the "one loss number per serial, locked at Tugallash" mechanism, unchanged in spirit from before).
- The hard gate relocating from dispatch to packing is the single most safety-critical change in this task — test it hardest, explicitly, at both the UI and RLS level.
- Delete the dead re-wash UI entirely (Ombor's void+resend flow, the amber "qayta yuvish" cards) — not disable, not hide behind a flag.
- Ship the schema+RLS+view changes as one migration rather than staging them, since the count of touched objects was small enough that splitting would only add risk of an inconsistent intermediate state.

**The model, precisely:** `wash_cycles` goes from `unique(serial, cycle_no)` to `unique(serial)` — `cycle_no` and `voided_at` dropped (the latter confirmed to have zero live writers even under the old system before removing it). `status`/`final_loss_pct`/`finalized_at` are unchanged in meaning. Critically, `lab_results.wash_cycle_id` is **untouched** — it still points at `wash_cycles.id`, still required on `scope='chiqim'` — and no unique constraint was ever forced on that relationship, so a reject-then-retest is just a second `lab_results` row against the *same* `wash_cycle_id`, with "current verdict" defined as the latest row by `created_at`. This is what makes "re-test, not re-cycle" fall out naturally from the existing shape rather than needing a new mechanism.

The `wash_cycles` row itself now gets minted at **first send to Moyka** (`OmborMoykaTab.tsx`'s `handleSend`, an `upsert(..., {onConflict: 'serial', ignoreDuplicates: true})` run alongside every `moyka_sends` insert, safe to repeat on every partial send) rather than at Tugallash — necessary because CHIQIM lab testing is now enterable from the moment material reaches Moyka, and `lab_results.wash_cycle_id` needs somewhere to point before that test can be recorded.

**The hard gate is a Postgres RLS `WITH CHECK`, not a UI convenience.** `finished_pallets`' `ombor_writes` INSERT policy now requires:
```sql
(select lr.verdict from lab_results lr join wash_cycles wc on wc.id = lr.wash_cycle_id
 where wc.serial = finished_pallets.serial and lr.scope = 'chiqim'
 order by lr.created_at desc limit 1) = 'o_tdi'
```
A scalar subquery ordered by `created_at desc limit 1`, deliberately — not `exists(...)` — because the requirement is "the *current* verdict is a pass," not "has ever passed once." A serial rejected after an earlier pass (impossible today, since nothing can un-pass through the UI, but the query is correct regardless) would not satisfy this. Matches SPEC's "hiding buttons is not security" principle: `OmborTayyorTab.tsx` also hides the pack button client-side as a convenience, but the real refusal happens here, proven directly (see Test 1 below).

**Reporting rewritten around the new model, not just patched (0035/0036):**
- `report_chiqim_rows`, `stock_on_hand_rows`, `wip_rows`, `yield_rows` (0035) — drop the `wash_cycle`/void-successor-via-`wash_cycle+1` logic that only made sense with numbered cycles. `wip_rows` loses its `qayta_yuvish_pending` CTE entirely (no more "cycle 2 hasn't started yet" state to report on). `yield_rows` drops the all\_\*/live\_\* split (nothing is voided under the new model, since a pallet cannot exist before its serial passes — there's no "voided vs current" distinction left to draw) and redefines `rewashed` as "had ≥1 reject in this serial's own lab history before its eventual pass," a boolean fact rather than a cycle count.
- `get_client_report`, `get_serial_passport`, `lab_turnaround_avg` (0036) — `get_client_report` drops `client_cycles`/`loss_period_cycles`/`cross_period_cycles`/`crossPeriodRewash` entirely (the whole concept of "raw sent in period A, a *second cycle* completing in period B" requires two cycles to exist). **Caught before applying:** an early draft of the loss arithmetic joined `finished_pallets` into the same CTE that carried `sent_actual_kg`, which would have multiplied the sent total by pallet count on any multi-pallet serial — fixed by splitting `loss_totals` (serial-level, summed once) from `loss_output` (pallet-level, joined separately), combined only in the final `loss_main` CTE. `lab_turnaround_avg` moved its anchor from `finished_pallets.received_date` to `min(moyka_sends.sent_date)`, matching the new trigger timing. `rahbar_monthly_trends`/`rahbar_client_ranking`/`rahbar_product_mix`/`rahbar_exceptions` were deliberately not touched — confirmed they only consume the preserved-name output columns of `yield_rows`/`report_*_rows`, not the removed keys.

**App-side rewrite:** `labVerdict.ts`'s `currentCycleLabStatus` became `currentLabStatus` (latest-row-per-`wash_cycle_id` resolution, sort+dedupe rather than a window function, since it's normally a handful of rows). `useMoykaSerials.ts`/`useMoykaOutput.ts` lost `activeCycle`/`isRewash` entirely — every send/pallet for a serial now belongs to the same single balance for its whole life. `useLaboratorChiqim.ts`/`LaboratorChiqimTab.tsx` rewritten for the new trigger timing (awaiting-list keyed by `wash_cycle_id`, `sentKg`/`sentDate` shown instead of a pallet count) — a rejected verdict correctly reappears in Window 1 highlighted (`rejected: boolean`, red "Rad etildi" text), not routed into the finished window (see bug below). `useLaboratorKirim.ts`/`KirimTahlilForm.tsx` gained the KIRIM-side equivalent trigger-timing change: every `kirim_lines` row is now visible from order creation, but only *enterable* once gate stage 1 (`gruzheny_kg`) exists — previously KIRIM lab testing was gated on a later confirmation step that no longer exists in this shape. `ChiqimTahlilForm.tsx` dropped its required pallet `<select>` (a pallet doesn't exist yet at test time under the new gate) for an optional free-text "Namuna manbai" field.

**Deleted outright (not disabled):** `src/lib/usePendingRewash.ts`, `src/lib/rewash.ts` + its test, `src/lib/activeCycles.ts`, `tests/e2e/rewash-hard-gate.spec.ts` (its whole premise — cycle 2 reopening dispatch via Ombor's own void+resend action — no longer exists), and every "sikl N" / "Qayta yuvish kerak" / "Qayta yuvishga yuborish" UI element across Kirim/Chiqim/reports screens (`KirimOrdersList.tsx`, `LaboratorTarixTab.tsx`, and others — confirmed via a full source grep before and after, see Test 3 below).

---

**Testing** (the user's own explicit follow-up task, run once the rewrite above was live).

**Test 1 — the hard gate, proven at both layers.** New spec `tests/e2e/lab-packing-hard-gate.spec.ts` (replacing the retired `rewash-hard-gate.spec.ts`): seeds two serials through KIRIM → gate → intake → Moyka send directly, then (a) confirms the pack action is *absent*, not disabled, on an untested serial, and separately attempts a **direct `finished_pallets` insert as Ombor via the dev `window.supabase` client** — proven refused by RLS, matching `/row-level security|policy/i`, not just a UI-level absence; (b) has Laborator pass one serial and reject the other; (c) confirms the rejected serial assigns no pallets, reappears in Laborator's own CHIQIM Window 1 highlighted "Rad etildi," and *still* cannot pack even after its sibling successfully did (the gate is per-serial, not per-batch). **1 passed reliably after two fixes below; see the 🚩 entries.**

**🚩 Real bug, found via this test: `useLaboratorChiqim.ts`/`useLaboratorKirim.ts` had the identical missing-guard gap as `useMoykaOutput.ts`/`useMoykaSerials.ts` above** — same one-shot mount fetch, same lack of any `cancelled`/request-id protection, both rewritten this session and both missing the convention every *other* one-shot-fetch hook in this codebase already follows. Symptom here was different and more disruptive than the empty-render case: with two overlapping `refresh()` calls in flight (StrictMode's mount double-invoke plus a manual `refresh()` from `handleTahlil` after the first of two verdicts in the same test), Laborator's CHIQIM list re-rendered repeatedly in quick succession, and a click on the second verdict button (`Qayta yuvish`) reliably hit "element was detached from the DOM, retrying" until the *test's own 180s timeout* — not a rare flake, reproduced identically on two separate isolated runs before the fix. Fixed with the same monotonic request-id guard as the Moyka hooks. Confirmed the fix, not a coincidence: before it, two consecutive isolated runs of this one spec both hung on the same click; after it, the click succeeds immediately and the spec passes cleanly on repeat runs.

**🚩 Real bug, found via this same test, after the above: `serialCard()` (the test's own locator helper) was ambiguous for longer than its one call site accounted for.** `ChiqimTahlilForm` renders its own "Seriya `<serial>`" summary in a nested `rounded-md` wrapper while open — same class of ambiguity this suite's own `serialCard()` was originally built to dodge (matching against the *closest* rounded-md ancestor of the serial's own chip, not a bare class selector), except here the form doesn't finish closing by the time a caller's very next assertion runs, since `handleTahlil`'s `setActiveTahlil(null)` + `refresh()` only commit after its async insert resolves and a click doesn't wait for that. Fixed in the test's own `testSerial()` helper: wait for `serialCard(serial)` to settle back to exactly one match (`toHaveCount(1)`, which — unlike `toBeVisible()` — doesn't require single-match to begin polling, so it's safe to call on an already-ambiguous locator) before returning control to the caller, rather than inferring "closed" from the clicked button alone (which turned out to still leave a brief window where the form's own summary block was the sole remaining match).

**Test 2 — loss computed at receipt, traced into both reports.** New spec `tests/e2e/lab-relocation-loss-verification.spec.ts`: a serial with a hand-checkable raw figure (gate 1100−100=1000kg) sent in full, passed, packed as one 850kg pallet — expected loss 150kg/15.0%. Confirms the Tugallash confirm dialog itself shows the number Ombor's own receipt produces (`150 kg · 15.0%`) before confirming, then traces the *same* number into `get_client_report` (`processedBreakdown.lossKg`/`lossPct`, JSONB, numeric) and `yield_rows` (`loss_kg`/`loss_pct`/`output_kg`, plain view, also numeric — see PostgREST note below). **1 passed, run twice back-to-back for reliability, both clean.**

**🚩 Real bug, found via this test, not hypothetical: `useMoykaOutput.ts`/`useMoykaSerials.ts` had no protection against overlapping `refresh()` calls.** Both hooks fetch once on mount via a bare `useEffect(() => { refresh() }, [refresh])`, no cancellation guard — every *other* one-shot-fetch hook in this codebase already carries a `cancelled`-on-cleanup guard (`useYieldRows.ts` and 7 others), but these two (rewritten this session) didn't. Under React StrictMode's dev-only double-invoke of the mount effect, two overlapping calls can race, and — since `refresh` is also called manually by mutation handlers (`handleSend`/`handleReceipt`/`handleTugallash`), a plain per-effect `cancelled` closure (the usual pattern here) can't cover every call site. Fixed with a monotonic request-id ref instead: only the most-recently-*started* call is ever allowed to commit state, regardless of which one resolves first. Symptom before the fix: after a fast role switch straight into a screen these hooks drive (e.g. Ombor's Tayyor Mahsulot right after a Laborator verdict), both windows could render completely empty — not a display bug, a stuck one: nothing ever re-triggers a one-shot mount fetch, so waiting longer never helped.

**🚩 Real bug, found via this test: `ClientReportTab.tsx` (and its Excel export) crashed unconditionally.** `report.raw.crossPeriodRewash.length > 0` — a leftover reference to the output key `get_client_report` (0036, above) removed entirely from its JSON shape. `report.raw.crossPeriodRewash` is `undefined` at runtime, so `.length` threw on *every* client report load and every Excel export, not a rare path. Fixed by removing the whole cross-period-rewash block from `ClientReportTab.tsx` and `clientReportExport.ts`, and the now-dead `ClientReportCrossPeriodRewash` type, `cycleNo` field, and `crossPeriodNote`/`cycle` labels from `clientReport.ts`/`clientReportLabels.ts`. This is exactly the kind of regression Test 2's own header comment already flagged this reporting layer as the highest-risk area for (this codebase has shipped one real loss-calculation bug here before) — caught by deliberately exercising the client report as part of tracing the loss figure, not by a targeted crash test.

**🚩 Real bug, found via this test: `OmborMoykaTab.tsx`'s send-to-Moyka step had no reliable success signal for a test to wait on.** The "+ Moykaga yuborish" button that opens the send form disappears the instant the form *opens* (`isActive` flips before any network call fires) — its absence proves nothing about whether the underlying `moyka_sends` insert actually completed. `lab-relocation-loss-verification.spec.ts`'s own first draft relied on this and was intermittently flaky as a result — sometimes the mutation's response hadn't landed before the test moved on to a role switch, and the write appears to have been cut short. Fixed in the test by wrapping the actual submit click in `page.waitForResponse()` for the `moyka_sends` POST and asserting it succeeded, rather than inferring success from an ambiguous button-count check.

**Test 3 — nothing wash-cycle-related left broken or orphaned.** A full source grep (`sikl |wash_cycle|cycle_no|activeCycle|isRewash|qayta_yuvish`) across `src/` surfaced ~20 files; each was checked against the *live* schema/RPC output (via direct SQL, not assumption) rather than trusted from the migration text alone, since the two bugs above were both cases of "should be safe" not holding up under a live check:
- **Genuinely broken, fixed:** the `ClientReportTab.tsx` crash above.
- **Genuinely dead, removed:** the `WashCycleFilter` "Yuvish sikli" dropdown in `ReportFilterBar.tsx`/`HisobotTab.tsx`/`reportQuery.ts` — the underlying `wash_cycle` column the filter compared against is always `null` now, so it could never match anything; `useReportQuery.ts` now passes `p_wash_cycle: null` directly (the RPC signature still requires the param, no default, so the wire shape is unchanged) rather than exposing a control that can't do anything. Same for `wip.ts`'s `qayta_yuvish_pending` `WipKind` — the DB view's own CTE producing it was already deleted in 0035, so the union member/label/sort-order entry were unreachable.
- **Misleading, fixed:** `SerialPassportModal.tsx`'s "Sikl {cycle.cycleNo}" heading and chiqim-lab-photo label, and `YieldTable.tsx`/`yieldExport.ts`'s "sikl {row.maxCycleNo}" badge/column — `cycleNo`/`max_cycle_no` are hardcoded to `1` server-side now (kept for JSON/row-shape compatibility with everything else still reading them), so these would have *always* displayed "Sikl 1," which reads as "there might be a cycle 2" to someone who doesn't know better. Replaced with what the data actually now means: a status label (Faol/Yakunlangan) in the passport, a plain "qayta tekshirilgan" (re-tested) badge in the yield table, "Ha"/"Yo'q" in its export — dropped the now-fully-unused `maxCycleNo`/`cycleNo` fields from `yield.ts`/`useYieldRows.ts`/`serialPassport.ts`/`clientReport.ts` entirely rather than leaving them typed-but-ignored.
- **Deliberately left alone, matching this session's own "defense in depth" precedent elsewhere:** `report_chiqim_rows`' `wash_cycle`/`void_successor_barcodes` columns and the `voidInfo`/"sikl N" void-successor display they feed (`reportQuery.ts`, `ChiqimRowDetail.tsx`, `HisobotTab.tsx`, `reportExport.ts`) — confirmed live that these columns still exist (emit `null`, not removed as keys), so the existing `num(row.wash_cycle) ?? 1` fallback is safe, not a crash risk. Nothing can produce a voided pallet through the UI anymore, so this code is unreachable in practice, but — like `stock_on_hand_rows`' `qayta_yuvish` bucket and `finished_pallets.status = 'bekor_qilingan'` before it — left correctly-derived rather than stripped, on the same reasoning already applied twice earlier in this session.
- **`full-chain.spec.ts` needed real restructuring, not just fixture updates** — see below.

**`full-chain.spec.ts`: sequence fix, plus a race-condition trail that took most of this task's own testing time to run down.** This pre-existing spec's own Laborator CHIQIM verdict ran *after* packing and Tugallash — valid under the old model (pack freely, test the completed cycle before Tugallash), backwards under the new hard gate. Restructured: the Laborator CHIQIM block (verdict, no more pallet `<select>`) now runs immediately after the Moyka send and before packing, with Ombor's own packing block gaining the role-switch it now needs (it used to inherit an already-logged-in Ombor session from the immediately-preceding step).

Getting this one spec green from there took far longer than expected, because the restructuring exposed a real, reproducible bug rather than a fixture mismatch: after the move, Ombor's own packing step (right after the Laborator excursion) intermittently rendered its target row absent, even though `moyka_sends`/`wash_cycles`/`lab_results` were confirmed correct via direct SQL at the moment of failure. Ruled out RLS (policy confirmed unchanged), token expiry (JWTs decoded live from the failing requests — valid, correctly scoped to Ombor), and "just needs more patience" (a flat 60s wait, 3× normal, still failed — proof nothing was ever going to retry on its own). Network capture *did* show something real along the way — `moyka_sends` occasionally queried carrying a stale JWT from the *previous* role, evidence of a pending fetch surviving past its component's own logout — but that wasn't the actual cause here, since a later, correctly-scoped request in the same trace still came back empty.

The actual cause, found by re-auditing every hook this session touched rather than the one screen that happened to be failing: `useLaboratorChiqim.ts` and `useLaboratorKirim.ts` had the identical missing-guard gap as `useMoykaOutput.ts`/`useMoykaSerials.ts` (see Test 1's own 🚩 entry above) — fixed the same way, with a monotonic request-id ref. That fix alone took this spec from failing at the packing step on every single run to passing it cleanly and consistently.

One further symptom surfaced past that point, not yet chased down: `LaboratorChiqimTab.tsx`'s own Window 3 ("Yakunlangan") sometimes shows a sulfured serial's row correctly moved out of "Sera kutilmoqda" (status genuinely `complete`) but without its verdict pill — `handleSera`'s single atomic `update({so2_mg_kg, status, verdict})` should never let one field of the same row read as committed while another doesn't, so this looks like the same general class of async-mutation/`refresh()`-ordering issue as the two bugs above, in a third location, rather than a new mechanism. Given this task's own explicit scope was Tests 1–3 on the wash-cycle relocation itself, and this is now clearly a broader, pre-existing pattern across several of this session's own rewritten hooks rather than anything specific to `full-chain.spec.ts`, it's flagged here rather than chased further: **worth a dedicated pass auditing every hook this session touched for the same missing-guard gap, and considering a shared `useOneShotFetch`-style helper so the protection is structural instead of copy-pasted per hook.** Test 1 and Test 2 above (the two specs this task actually asked for) show no trace of any of this and pass reliably on repeat runs.

**Manual steps (cannot be automated in this environment — no Bluetooth hardware, no phone camera):**
1. On a real device with the P1 printer paired: as Ombor, pack a pallet for a *passed* serial and confirm the sticker actually prints at 40×30mm with legible text (barcode, serial, type, calibre, weight, owner) and a scannable QR code.
2. Scan that printed QR with a real phone camera (or the app's own `BarcodeCameraScanner`, `OmborChiqimTab`'s scan zone) and confirm it resolves to the same barcode2 value shown on screen (`stripBarcode2Prefix(data.barcode2)` is what's encoded — the barcode2 ID with its `PLT-` prefix stripped, not the full string).
3. Attempt the same pack action for an untested or rejected serial and confirm no print path is reachable at all (no button, matching Test 1's UI-level assertion) — the RLS refusal from Test 1 is the real backstop if this is ever bypassed.

**Verification.** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npm run lint` clean (same one pre-existing `AuthProvider.tsx` fast-refresh warning, unrelated). Unit suite 78/78. `lab-packing-hard-gate.spec.ts` and `lab-relocation-loss-verification.spec.ts` (Tests 1 and 2, this task's own two deliverables) each pass reliably on repeat isolated runs; `chiqim-undo-scan.spec.ts` and `report-effective-qty-parity.spec.ts` pass. `full-chain.spec.ts` gets cleanly past its restructured sequence and the packing-step race fixed above, then hits the further, not-yet-chased-down `Window 3` verdict-pill symptom described above on some runs. This environment also independently confirmed a pre-existing, already-documented characteristic (see this file's own `playwright.config.ts` header comment): individual specs that pass cleanly in isolation can still flake when run deep in a full sequential sweep — observed once on `lab-packing-hard-gate.spec.ts` itself during a full-suite run, unrelated to the fixes above.

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** opening-stock seeding, the cut line, the full coverage-matrix scenarios beyond Tests 1–3 above (the user's own words: "I run those myself").

## 2026-07-29 — Boshqaruv moved to Menejer: Diqqat talab, Sozlamalar, Foydalanuvchilar

**Context:** Rahbar's only write surface (§6.4 Boshqaruv: product-settings master data + threshold admin) plus its Diqqat talab exceptions view (§6.2) moved to Menejer wholesale — nav/routes removed from Rahbar, added to Menejer, content of all three screens unchanged. Explicitly framed by the user as a move, not a duplication: after this task Rahbar has no access to any of the three, and Menejer has full access.

**Investigation before touching anything, per the task's own explicit gate.** Diqqat talab turned out to need no RLS change at all: `rahbar_exceptions()` and the three other dashboard RPCs it and RahbarHome share were already `grant execute to authenticated` (0030) — role-named, not role-restricted, so Menejer could already call them; only the route stood in the way. Sozlamalar's four non-owners tables (`product_categories`/`product_types`/`calibres`/`settings_limits`) were genuinely rahbar-only via RLS — moving the screen without a policy change would have rendered it for Menejer with every save failing silently against RLS, exactly the risk the task called out. `owners` already had a Menejer grant from the original §3.3 build (`menejer_owners_insert`/`update`), unchanged by this task. Foydalanuvchilar turned out not to be an RLS case at all: `UsersAdminPage.tsx` never writes to `profiles` from the browser — it calls the `admin-users` Edge Function, which uses the `service_role` key (bypasses RLS entirely) and enforces its own hardcoded `callerProfile.role !== 'rahbar'` check. The real gate to move was Edge Function code, not a Postgres policy — stated precisely to the user rather than loosely calling it "RLS" throughout.

**Two things confirmed with the user before any write, both explicitly framed as open questions rather than assumed:**
1. **Does Rahbar keep the underlying write grants as an unreachable-via-UI safety net, or lose them entirely?** Surfaced the real stakes first: Rahbar has zero write policy on any operational table already (§2.12) — write access existed only inside Administration. Revoking the Administration grants too, not just the routes, makes Rahbar **fully read-only app-wide**, a real architectural shift worth deciding consciously, not as a side effect of a nav reshuffle. **User chose: revoke entirely.** Rahbar now has 6 remaining nav destinations, all read-only reporting/oversight screens.
2. **Should Menejer's Foydalanuvchilar access be full parity with Rahbar's today, or restricted from touching rahbar-role accounts?** Flagged a real, non-obvious security implication first: the Edge Function's authorization is all-or-nothing per **caller** role, with no concept of scoping by **target** role — an unrestricted grant lets Menejer `create-user` a brand-new rahbar account (role is a field the caller sends, checked only against the 5-role allow-list) and `reset-password` on any account including a real Rahbar's, unscoped. **User chose: full parity, no restriction** — accepted the tradeoff explicitly rather than having it default in silently.

**RLS applied** (`supabase/migrations/0037_menejer_admin_move.sql`, applied live and captured to a local migration file in the same session): dropped `rahbar_admin_{product_categories,product_types,calibres}_{insert,update}`, `rahbar_admin_settings_limits_update`, `rahbar_admin_owners_{insert,update}`; created the `menejer_admin_*` equivalents on the first four (settings_limits keeps its existing insert-policy-free shape — thresholds are still seeded by migration only, never created via UI). `owners` needed no new Menejer policy (already existed); just the Rahbar one dropped. **`rahbar_admin_profiles` (FOR ALL) dropped with no Menejer replacement** — confirmed via grep that no browser code writes to `profiles` directly (`useProfile.ts`/`useProfileNames.ts` are read-only), so nothing exercises it; a broad FOR ALL grant here would additionally let Menejer self-promote via a raw `profiles.role` update, a real privilege-escalation path nothing in this task required opening. Verified live before and after via direct `pg_policies` query, not assumed from the migration text.

**Edge Function changed and redeployed:** `admin-users/index.ts`'s caller check (`callerProfile.role !== 'rahbar'`) swapped to `'menejer'`, error message and doc comment updated to match, deployed via the Supabase MCP directly (this session's environment can reach the platform API, unlike the sandboxed CLI environment noted in the original 2026-07-13 entries — confirmed live, version bumped 13→14, status ACTIVE). `ALLOWED_ROLES` (the target-role allow-list for `create-user`) intentionally left untouched — full parity was the confirmed decision.

**Route gating confirmed genuine, not just hidden nav.** All three routes lived inside `/rahbar`'s single `RoleRoute allow={['rahbar']}`-guarded block in `App.tsx`; removing their nested `<Route>` entries removes the paths outright (they fall through to the catch-all role-home redirect), not just their nav links. Re-added under `/menejer`'s own `RoleRoute allow={['menejer']}` block, same gating pattern every other role already uses. Grepped `tests/` and the rest of `src/` for all three screens first — zero e2e coverage, nothing else in the codebase assumed Rahbar-only — so the move carried no hidden blast radius.

**Files relocated, not just re-routed** — `git mv`, so history follows: `src/pages/rahbar/RahbarExceptionsTab.tsx` → `src/pages/menejer/MenejerExceptionsTab.tsx`, `RahbarSettingsTab.tsx` → `MenejerSettingsTab.tsx` (both renamed to match), `ProductCategoriesSection.tsx`/`ProductTypesSection.tsx`/`CalibresSection.tsx`/`ThresholdsSection.tsx` (same names, new folder). Kept at the same `pages/<role>/` depth deliberately — same relative-import shape, zero import-path edits needed inside them, and consistent with this codebase's own convention that `components/` is reserved for pieces reused by *multiple* consumers (like `ClientsSection.tsx`, shared by Mijozlar and Sozlamalar) while single-consumer admin sections live under their owning role's `pages/` folder — these four are single-consumer (`MenejerSettingsTab.tsx` alone) both before and after the move, just under a different role. `UsersAdminPage.tsx` needed no move — already lived at the role-neutral `src/pages/admin/`. Stale "Rahbar-only" comments inside the moved files corrected to avoid misleading a future reader; historical mockup/rationale comments otherwise left intact.

**One accepted overlap, left alone rather than silently redesigned, since the task scoped out screen content changes:** Menejer now has both `/menejer/mijozlar` (existing, no-deactivate clients-only screen) and `/menejer/sozlamalar` → `ClientsSection allowDeactivate` (moved as-is, deactivate included) — two client-management surfaces with different permissions for the same role. This is a direct consequence of moving Sozlamalar unmodified while Mijozlar already existed independently, not something introduced by redesigning either screen; noted for awareness rather than fixed, since "only where they live and who can reach them" was the explicit scope.

**Verification:** `npx tsc -b --noEmit` clean, `npm run lint` clean (same one pre-existing unrelated `AuthProvider.tsx` warning), unit suite 78/78 unchanged. Live-browser-verified: logged in as Rahbar, confirmed all three items gone from nav and confirmed direct navigation to `/rahbar/sozlamalar` (and the other two) redirects to `/rahbar` rather than rendering anything. Logged in as Menejer, confirmed all three appear in nav, confirmed a Sozlamalar threshold save, a Diqqat talab list load, and a Foydalanuvchilar user creation each succeed with no RLS refusal. Full Playwright suite run once.

**Docs updated in the same pass:** SPEC.md §2.12 (RLS bullets + the "write only in Administration" line), §3.3 (rewritten — now covers the full Boshqaruv scope, not clients-only), §6.2/§6.4 (kept as historical record with a MOVED pointer to §3.3, not deleted or renumbered — existing "§6.2"/"§6.4" references elsewhere in this file and in code comments still resolve to something accurate this way), and a new v1.22 changelog row.

**Out of scope, not touched (per the task):** the content/behavior of the three screens themselves, the Playwright `.env.test` role accounts, the cutting line.

## 2026-07-30 — Correcting mistaken entries: date/plate/driver editable from KIRIM/CHIQIM second windows

**Context:** A wrong date (the driving example) or plate/driver entered on a KIRIM order or CHIQIM request previously had no fix short of living with it — neither table has a delete mechanism, and CHIQIM's only existing mutation was void (kill the whole request). Added a scoped edit action to both second windows (`KirimOrdersList.tsx`, `FinishedChiqimList.tsx`), limited to fields nothing downstream reads for math.

**Investigation before coding, per the task's own gate.** Live schema/RLS checked directly (not the migration history, which is stale in places — `kirim_orders.serial` from 0003 was superseded by `order_id` in 0009/0011).
